EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version European forewordThis document presents a Fare Information European Profile for Rail of the European Technical Specification CEN/TS 16614 known as “NeTEx”. NeTEx provides a framework for specifying communications and data exchange protocols for organizations wishing to exchange scheduled Information relating to public transport operations.NeTEx is made up of three parts defining a single European Standard, which provides a complete exchange format for public transport networks, timetable description and fare information.Part 1 is the description of the public transport network topology exchange format. It also contains use cases shared with part 2, and modelling rules and the description of a framework shared by all parts.Part 2 is the description of the scheduled timetables exchange format.Part 3 is the description of the fare information exchange format.Part 1 is fully standalone, parts 2 and 3 rely on part 1.The XML schema can be downloaded from https://github.com/NeTEx-CEN/NeTEx and https://transmodel-cen.eu/index.php/netex/ provides available guidance on its use, example XML files, and case studies of national and local deployments.This document is a so called fare profile, defining those elements of NeTEx to be used for the exchange of fare information.This document is highly technical, and special care has been taken on keeping the text readable. This has been done through a set of editorial rules enhancing usual CEN writing rules as presented in 4.1120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version IntroductionPublic transport services rely increasingly on information systems to ensure reliable, efficient operation andwidely accessible, accurate passenger information. These systems are used for a range of specific purposes:setting schedules and timetables; managing vehicle fleets; issuing tickets and receipts; providing real-time information on service running, and so on.This European Technical Specification specifies a Profile of NeTEx for fare information, applicable in particular to rail transport. NeTEx is intended to be used to exchange information between Public Transportorganisations systems containing scheduled public transport data. It can also be seen as a complement to the SIRI (Service Interface for Real-time Information) standard (EN 15531 series), as SIRI needs reference data exchange in the scope of NeTEx before any possible real-time exchange.Well-defined, open interfaces have a crucial role in improving the economic and technical viability of public transport Information Systems of all kinds. Using standardized interfaces, systems can be implemented as discrete pluggable modules that can be chosen from a wide variety of suppliers in a competitive market, rather than as monolithic proprietary systems from a single supplier. Interfaces also allow the systematic automated testing of each functional module, vital for managing the complexity of increasing large and dynamic systems. Furthermore, individual functional modules can be replaced or evolved, without unexpected breakages of obscurely dependent function.NeTEx improves a number of features of public transport information and service management: Interoperability – the standard will facilitate interoperability between information processing systems of the transport operators by: (i)introducing common architectures for message exchange; (ii)introducing a modular set of compatible information services for real-time vehicle information; (iii)using common data models and schemas for the messages exchanged for each service; (iv)introducing a consistent approach to data management.Technical advantages include the following: reusing a common communication layer shared with SIRI for all the various technical services enables cost-effective implementations, and makes the standard readily extensible in future.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Profiles are an additional document to the standard which specifies additional rules for implementation in agiven context. The profile contains information such as:Details of the objects used in an exchangeDetails on the options proposed by the standardDetails on optional elementsPrecision on the codifications to be used…The reason behind the detail of the profile specifications is that it facilitates subsequent implementations. Adeveloper intending to implement a certain service or type of service in a given environment would just need to access the corresponding standard profiles, and implement the options and parameters already specified in the document. The current document is a European NeTEx Profile for rail fare information (European Fare Profile for Rail (EFIP)) , designed to carry the minimum information set to feed passenger information services in a European wide context for rail. EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 1ScopeThis technical specification is a profile of the NeTEx CEN/TS 16614 series. It focuses on information relevant for exchanging  fare  information to support trip planners and pricing engines. This version of the European Fare Information Profile (EFIP) has a specific focus on rail transport modes, but remains a part of the future European Fare Information Profile. The advent of mobile technology is transforming both the way fares are delivered, and the products that are offered for example with mobile apps, account based ticketing with consumption based capping. The profile includes capabilities to describe advanced electronic fares.The overall NeTEx standard is dedicated to the exchange of planned data (network topology, timetable and fare information) and is based on Transmodel V6 (EN 12986)  which sets out an extensive conceptual modeldescribing many different functional areas of passenger information and transport operation in addition to fares. This profile focuses specifically on the exchange of fare data, in particular for rail operation.

Interoperability Directive’). The geographical coverage is defined as the Union rail system.1.1ProfilesStandards are by their nature and definition, consensus documents. In the case of the CEN and ISOstandards, and more particularly in the field of traffic applications, these are established at an internationallevel, with a significant number of different stakeholders involved in their development and discussion. Thismeans that CEN and ISO standards take into account requirements that are much wider than a typical localimplementations of such standards. The standard writing procedure itself makes updates of the documenta long process, so standards are written to have the longest possible life and stability.In practice a given implementation will target only a subset of a full standard and further more will typicallymake certain assumptions, impose further constraints on identifiers and other aspects of theimplementation that reduce the work of implementation.  The rules as to the contents and constraints canbe formally described as a ‘profile’.Additionally, profiles can be accompanied by the definition of specific testing procedures to assess theconformance of the implemented solution with the profile.A profile remains 100% compliant with the full standard.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version From a practical point of view, profiles can be seen as an implementation guidelines. Instead of having toface the challenge of analysing the whole standard, discover the relevant parts for a given application, andthen adjusting optional values and parameters for the intended application, profiles can be specified toaddress the needs of a particular application and then used for any subsequent similar initiative.Fare structures and fare products vary greatly in their complexity; furthermore, some are very commonwhile others are rare and there are many 'edge cases’. While even very complex fare structures can beexpressed in NeTEx, the majority of fares are more straightforward. EFIP (European Fare Profile) focuses oncommonly found tariff structures and products such as point to point long-distance,  fares,  urban zonalfares etc.  1.2European Fare Information ProfileThe European Fare Information Profile (EFIP) complements the European Passenger Information Profile(EPIP) . Common framework and Network elements that are already described in the EPIP are notdescribed.  The European Fare Information Profile is concerned  only with  data  relevant to feed passengerinformation service with fare informations.  It  excludes operational  and back office information..Typical use cases for the European Fare Information Profile are: Provide fare products & fare prices as open data for third party use (e.g.trip  planners,  fare engines) Describe available fare products and their eligibility conditions. Relate fare products to network and timetabled journeys so trip planners can compute fare products and fare prices for trips, show available products for an area, etc. Allow the separate exchange of prices from fare structures & products. Expose a justification of the fare (Distance, discounts etc) Support both machine readable & human readable representation of validity parameters needed to understand the access rights to  travel  granted and to   control fares.Include information about how/where products can be boughtInclude fares valid for specific and multiple operators.  Provide  information about fare routes, zones and networks for maps and online visualisations.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version EFIP requires; (a)Use of some common NeTEx Framework features (NeTEx Part1) and EPIP(b)Use of use some common NeTEx network and journey components (NeTEx Part1 & NeTEx Part2),and EPIP  .(c)Use of   the NeTEx Fare model (NeTEx Part3).This profile has been designed to be as concise as possible and focus on commonly agreed needs to fulfilthe requirements expressed in the Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/253 of 6 February 2026on a technical specification relating to the telematics subsystem of the rail system in the European Unionfor interoperability of data sharing in rail transport (TEL TSI) and repealing Regulations (EU) No 454/2011(TAP TSI) and (EU) No 1305/2014 (TAF TSI). 1.3Type of fare covered by the profileThis document sets a profile for use of the NeTEX schema to describe common European fares.  The profileaims to cover basic fare data for commonly found fares, covering all modes of transport with particularattention to the rail transport, including international and cross-border tariffs.Fare structures: point-to-point, zonal, distance, unit stage and flat fares.Product types: oTrips single and return trips, carnets of trips, oPeriod Passes; time passes, day passes , carnets of day passes,  season tickets,oSupplement products: seat reservations, luggage transport: pets transport, bicycletransport, accompagnied car transport etc User types: adult child, senior , concession, group, family, etcAnonymous and Account based ticketing, Fare capping by period,Sales offers: s: single  products and product bundles,Materalisation: ticketpapaer, plain-paper, smartcard, SMS  and electronic,Pricing: absolute,  derived and dynamic prices (yield managed prices).Information on sales and conditionsDistribution channles, fulfilment means,  payment means ,Exchange and refund conditions.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

This means that 'fares’ are described in a first place through access rights to transport services (by sets of rules and related parameters), 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version fare products and a fare offer are defined in a second step (sometimes as combination of fareproducts provided for example by different operators),then pricing mechanisms are applied to the fare offer elements to provide the price.To fully understand this specification it is necessary to take into account this ‘philosophy’.1.6Data consistency and QualityOne of the goals of a profile is to simplify and enhance interoperability. But interoperability will not be achieved if the data just pretend to be compliant to the profile without fully following it and being of good quality (the data can be read, is consistent, and describes the reality).Therefore the profile comes together with a set of consistency and quality checking rules specifically designed for NeTEx under this profile. Following these rules will not allow to guarantee a 100% quality of any dataset, but will prevent most of the quality issues usually encountered (when there is a way to automatically control it: for example a rule can control that a SERVICE JOURNEY has PASSING TIMEs, but will not be able to detect that a STOP PLACE name is not the right one).2Normative referencesSee section B.3.3Terms and definitionsSee section B.24Presentation conventionsThe conventions used in this document are the ones used in the main NeTEx document.4.1Recap on Transmodel and NeTEx  naming and presentation conventionsNeTEx follows certain conventions for presentation of technical terms:Transmodel conceptual model elements are shown in UPPER CASE, for example “LINE”, “SCHEDULED STOP POINT”.Concrete XML elements are shown in bold italic, for example, Line. Compound names are camel cased without a space and are never pluralised, e.g. “ScheduledStopPoint”.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Entity and class names are capitalised, as are properties of an element that are implemented as a XML child element, for example, “ScheduledStopPoint / Name”. Properties that are implemented as XML attributes are lower cased, for example “id”, “order “. One-to-one relationships that are implemented as versioned references are shown as simple attribute names, for example ScheduledStopPointRef, ZoneRef. Sometimes these are qualified by a name to distinguish them for example ParentZoneRef, FromPointRef, ToPointRef, etc.One-to-many relationships that are implemented as collections are lower cased and are always in the plural, for example, stopPoints, vehicleJourneys.Data types are shown in italic, built-in xml types are shown in lowerCamelcase, for example dateTime, normalizedString, integer. NeTEx Data types are shown in UpperCamelCase, e.g. LengthType, DirectionTypeEnumeration. Compound names are camel cased without a space, for example ScheduledStopPointIdType.4.2Use of colourElements in Transmodel and NeTEx  UML diagrams are coloured according to their functional area. Additionally, in the EFIP abstract super classes are greyed out to rely on the concrete classes corresponding to the XML tag instances. 4.3Additional presentation conventionsA few additional conventions were added for the specific context of the profile:Table 2 – Additional presentation convention«FK»dataSourceRefDataSourceIdType0:1Data system which originated data instance.createdxsd:dateTime0:1Date and time of creation of ENTITY.This is a specific addition (rule, constraint, etc.) forthe profile.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.euNo highlight means that it isidentical or similar (same meaning)to the original NeTEx textLight Grey highlight emphasis anadditional rule or constraint addedto the original NeTEx text

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version changedxsd:dateTime0:1Date and time of last change to ENTITY.Note: Unselected attributes (highlighted in blue) can still be used since they will still be valid for the NeTEx XSD. However they will not be read and understood by the receiver. Moreover they may be the source of issues for those deciding to use a reduced or optimised XSD. Therefore, using them should result of an agreement between involved partner.4.3.1Caution on terminologyTransmodel & NeTEx use the same systematic vocabulary of terms for specific domain concepts (LINE, NETWORK, VEHICLE, TRANSPORT MODE, etc). Often the usage will be straightforward and the meaning intuitive (POINT OF INTEREST, TIMING POINT, FARE ZONE, etc.).In some cases, these terms may appear non-intuitive or cumbersome (e.g. ACCESS RIGHT PARAMETER ASSIGNMENT, TEMPLATE SERVICE JOURNEY, SALES OFFER PACKAGE ELEMENT, etc) and it requires some effort to acquire a ready understanding of their precise meaning.In other cases, where the term corresponds to just one of several senses that a term has in common vernacular use (for example LINE, ROUTE, DIRECTION, TARIFF, FARE PRODUCT, etc), care should be taken to avoid confusion with the other vernacular senses.4.4XML schema technologyNeTEx uses W3C XML schema, a very well- established mainstream technology for describing data sets. W3C schema are used to describe documents for many of the most commonly exchanged data sets (HTML, Microsoft Office, etc) globally. Although relatively verbose compared to unvalidated formats (such as csv or JSON), it has a number of important advantages for the long-term management of complex data sets.Data quality: The schema provides a detailed description of the expected content which can be used to perform many basic validity checks for data quality and referential integrity automatically.Flexibility: The self-describing nature of schema documents makes it possible build applications that use data selectively, for many different purposes, and to evolve the schema without breaking backwards compatibility.Machine-readable- the self-describing format can be used to automate most of the legwork of implementation, building parser bindings that present the data to a program interface in a ready to use form.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.euBlue highlight means that thisattribute is not selected for theprofile

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Widespread adoption: there are a wide range of tools and many people with the necessary technology skill sets to draw on.5Overview of access rights and  component-based fares5.1Use of NeTEx for Fare dataNeTEx relies on the European Reference Data Model also called Transmodel, developed within CEN and which is a European norm (EN12896). Transmodel provides a rich representational data model for describing both simple and complex fare products and their prices. Fare products are assembled from a set of low-level reusable components, building on other common data sets that describe the stops, lines and timetabled services of a transport network. Here we explain a few key principles of the Transmodel/NeTEx representation before discussing the EFIP scope further.5.2Access rights and the Transmodel/NeTEx representationThe essential characteristic of the Transmodel fares   data model (EN12896-5) is that it is based on access rights i.e. the right to consume travel and other services, rather than directly on fare products. As mentioned above, NeTEx adopts this approach and for this reason, the explanations provided use UPPER CASE for the involved data entities and the text below is largely based on EN12896-5. An access right granted to a customer is a part of a service that a user is entitled to consume, and of which the service provider (or another organisation) is able to control the consumption. Familiar examples include:  to ride between two stops on a service provided by a specific operator,  to travel within a specific zone for a specified period of time, to make a short bus journey of up to three stops, to have meal onboard,to sleep in a couchette, to take a pet or a bicyle as bagage, etc, etc . Various access rights may be combined in order to form immaterial “fare products” (for example, a “single ride” granted by a fare product called “simple ticket” or “multiple trips during one month” granted by a fare product called “monthly pass”), which are marketable sets of access rights.One or more  fare products may be associated with a “travel document” and materialised as sales offer packages that may be purchased.(e.g. a paper single ticket allowing only a “single ride” or an electronic card containing various fare products, or a mobile app that holds a ticket purchased in electronic form including a token such as bar code that can be used to validate its consumption).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Combinations of fare products and travel documents , with options selected for the customers’ specific trip are sold to customers as “customer purchase  packages”. Each sold package is part of an individual “contract” with a particular customer.A variety of fare prices may be attached to a particular access right, each distinguishing a price that applies under particular conditions. The same access right may be sold in a wide range of marketable combinations, and access right can cover not just use of the transport network, but other consumable services such as meals, use of wifi, parking etc. The advantage of using the concept of an access right is that it allows a flexible composition of many different types of fare products, a clear definition of what is being priced, the rules for consuming access rights and the commercial conditions related to the sale.  The Transmodel / NeTEx component approach can be contrasted with classical   models for representing fares, which typically conflate the different concerns of access right, fare product, fare price, type of travel document etc,  in a smaller number of elements that cannot be recombined or reused.In summary: the concept of an access right allows tariff elements to be treated as reusable components which can be assembled in different ways to create different products.5.3Defining Tariffs with Transmodel/NeTExTariffs and Fare products are described in several steps (Figure 2), summarised as follows; a)The relevant elements of the transport network (stops, tariff zones, etc,) and timetabled services (e.g. specific journeys with fare restrictions, etc). which may be used in a product are identified.b)A tariff structure is defined in terms of the spatial and temporal elements that are available to access, along with any other features or aspects of use that are subject to differentiated pricing (e.g.class of use, eligible types of user, peak or off-peak consumption, etc.).c)Sets of access rights (e.g. rights to use a zone, or travel between stops, etc.) are defined as validable elements.d)Fare products are defined by combining the sets of access rights with additional conditions such as commercial conditions for purchase and refund, etc.e)Fare products are combined into sales offer packages that describe the ticket media on which the products are available, along with any additional commercial conditions and the permitted sales channels.f)Prices for the allowed combinations of packages and fare product options are defined.g)Purchases of the fare offer elements can be recorded as sales transactions, each recording a set of fare product choices and a purchase price. (This last step is outside of the scope of the EFIP).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version There are uniform common mechanisms for organizing and versioning sets of data, and for describing common such as validity conditions, etc.EFIPEFIP adopts additional specifc conventions for organising tariffs and fare elements  in a document.A fundamental principle of the Transmodel/  NeTEx approach is that prices are held separate to the elements that they price.Another important principle is to reuse existing data elements from other Transmodel  ‘layers’ wherever possible – thus for example, the same stop data is used for timetables and tariffs. Figure 1 – Transmodel/NeTEx Fare Specification modules with examplesFor each of the different stages of defining a fare (access rights, fare product conditions of use, offer commercial conditions etc), NeTEx provides a set of standardized reusable components. 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version The definition of a given fare product may contain multiple options. The resulting fare   product definition   will include all the possible alternatives that someone buying the   fare   product  (s)   based on the tariff   might select. For example, a standard metro ticket might be available with different options adult or child; peak or off-peak; zone 1 or zone 2 etc(as opposed say to having different fare products for different combinations of options).When a customer wants to know the price of a given fare product, a travel specification provides the set of specific choices (out of the available alternatives) for their intended consumption that determine the accessrights, parameters and price of the fare. For example, ‘An adult trip in zone 1’, ‘A child trip in zone 2’ might represent different choices from the same product that has separate adult/child and zone1/zone2 options. Any set of specific choices can be given a price. This may either be done statically (i.e. by holding a separateprice for every permitted combination), or by holding a base price for certain of the tariff components and apply a set of derivation rules to calculate the prices (for example child price is half the adult price).Figure 2 – Transmodel/NeTEx Fare Specification modules120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

The figure, as elsewhere in the presentation material, makes use of “infographics”; that is, a consistent icon is assigned to each concept and used to aid the understanding of complex material by readers.5.5Combining access rightsA further visualisation of how fares are specified with Transmodel/NeTEx components is given in the following diagram (UPPER CASE is used for Transmodel data elements)Figure 3 – Composing a product definition from fare structure elementsThe figure above shows that to express several options for one access right (e.g a VALIDABLE ELEMENT) several types of parameters may be assigned (PARAMETER ASSIGNMENT).  120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version This figure shows several data elements (in UPPER CASE) which are further described in chapter 8 of this document A TARIFF is a set of FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs that define access rights in terms of network elements, temporal components and other parameters.  The access right and FARE STRUCTIURE ELEMENTs can be given individual prices if appropriate;A VALIDABLE ELEMENT groups a set of FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTS that can be checked (e.g. a bus ride in Zone 1) when the passenger is travelling.;A FARE PRODUCT is the set of choices of elements and parameters that may be consumed as VALIDABLE ELEMENTS and that are marketed to the user as named product.;A SALES OFFER PACKAGE bundles up one or more FARE PRODUCTs as priced offering that may be purchased,  and also specifies the media upon which it is available (e.g. paper, magstripe  mobile app, When a user purchases a product (a  sales offer element), he chooses a permitted combination of specific options. 4There may be further conditions that apply to a specific VALIDABLE ELEMENT, FARE PRODUCT or SALES OFFER PACKAGE. These also can be described using the same mechanism of parameter assignment. Transmodel/NeTEx has a general-purpose VALIDITY PARAMETER ASSIGNMENT element that allows further complex validity conditions to be constructed using simple elements related to for example to space or time (for instance ‘access right valid for metro line A form 10 p.m. to 2 a.m.).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Figure 5 – Specifying complex conditions for a product5.6Combining Multiple TariffsOften multiple tariffs will apply to an itinerary. For examplei.the simple single trip fares, ii.zonal fares for an operator’s day and season  pass products and iii.tariffs marketed with other fare products, for example related to different modes (train only, train +bus, etc).here may also be products from multiple different operators for different modes .In particular, where multiple tariffs and fare products are applicable to an itinerary, Transmodel/NeTEx do not say which is “best”; it merely allows them all to be specified. Typically, it is the task of journey planning or fare finding software (whether in an online service, ticket machine or station office on-line terminal) to decide which fares it wishes to show as available and in which order they should be presented. Various criteria are possible – cheapest for the user; most flexible for the user; most profitable for the operator; satisfying regulatory strictures, etc.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version  Figure 6 – Combining multiple tariffsIn order for trip planners or fare engines to make use of multiple tariffs,  the fare structure, fare products and prices for each tariff should be separately defined, with all relevant conditions and restrictions. For their access rights they should reference the same network elements, for example stops and tariff zones as the trip. They should be packaged as separate XML documents that can be updated and distributed independently of each other.  A trip planner/fare engine can then use these structure definitions to find thefare offers that are relevant for a given travel specification.5.7Other Introductory comments5.7.1Organisation of elements in profile documentsDifferent transport operators will choose to market their fares in different ways. For example, one operatormight have separately named fare products for Adult  and   Child , with different price options for peak and off peak travel.  Another operator might have four differently named farevproducts:  Adult Peak, Adult off peak, Child Peak, and Child off peak.  Essentially these are offering the same access rights, but are just 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version marketed in different groupings. The strength of a component based approach is that it allows great flexibility in describing the marketed packages. The same basic components may be used in a myriad of ways.  However, in order to reduce the complexity for an implementor it is important to limit the ways in which components are combined to a practical subset and to organise the elements in a uniform manner. This is discussed further below.5.7.2Handling large data setsPublic transport data sets can be technically challenging to standardise, not only because they cover a complex domain, but also because they can be large, requiring consideration of the scaling implications for processing. Furthermore, data and subsets of data are often exchanged repeatedly between many stakeholders, necessitating a careful coordination of versions in order to achieve coherence (with necessarymetadata to achieve this). The NeTEx framework includes mechanisms to assist with the management of large datasets including:Frames: A container mechanism that allows data to be modularised into coherent subsets. For example, “all the Season ticket products for a specific operator”, or “all the Network elements describing a line and its stops”. There are different types of version frames for exchanging different types of data (Figure 7).Groups: Groups of elements of a specific type may be grouped in named lists, allowing their repeated reuse as sets, and the assignment of common properties. For example, groups of sales offerings may be given shared properties, so that many similar products can be efficiently defined just by their additional properties.Versioning and Validity conditions. Fare management especially for multi-operator products will typically involve the exchange and aggregation of data from multiple stakeholders. There will be successive versions of data over time with updates having a specific validity period.Systematic global identifiers. In order to integrate diverse data sets a persistent, globally unique way of identifying elements of any type is needed. NeTEx employs a powerful uniform system of code spaces based on W3C namespaces.Referential integrity checks: Typically, a given data set will require a coherent set of elements all to be present in order to be usable. NeTEx makes extensive use of XML’s built in validity-checking mechanisms to validate internal cross-references within a document to ensure completeness. For external references to entities in other documents a uniform approach (including versioned references) is used that facilitates programmatic checking.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version  Figure 7 – Types of Version frame6EFIP Functional scope This part describes the functional scope for a EFIP Fares profile, in particular for rail.6.1Primary use case for EFIPFare and tariff data may be used in different stages of fare management and in many different ways. For example, to plan and set tariff structures and prices; to create published printed fare tables; to provision ticket machines; to inform journey planning engines of trip prices; to support validation and control of passengers, etc. The Transmodel and NeTEx specifications set out a number of potential use cases that characterise the requirements.Note however that the EFIP is concerned primarily with just one specific use case;

To provide fare products & fare prices as distributed open data for third party use (by journey planners, fare calculation engines, etc.).The content of the distributed data shall:120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version •Describe available fare products and their eligibility conditions for different types of users (adult, child, student, etc),•Relate fare products to the bus network and the timetabled journeys so trip planners can compute fare products and fare prices for trips, show the tariff zones and the available products for an area, etc,•Describe available travel documents (printed tickets, smart cards mobile apps) and any tariff differentiation between them),•Allow the separate exchange of prices independently of the tariff structures & products.•Expose a justification of the fare (by distance, stage, etc), •Support both machine readable & human readable representation of validity conditions and terms of carriage. (NB mostly URL to document for MMTIS ),•Include information about how/where products can be bought,•Include fares valid for specific and multiple operators,•Be usable in cross border routes, such as in Ireland,•Include metadata about the document contents, eg operator, type of fare.6.2Standard fares versus best value faresPassenger information applications may be concerned to answer two quite distinct questions from passengers that differ substantially in their data requirements:What is the typical cost of a standard fare? A useful indication of this can usually be obtained just by considering single use, one-way fares for adults and children that are available for a trip. Although cheaper fares may well exist, the standard fare sets a limit as to the likely cost.What is the best value fare? To answer this correctly, a knowledge of all the available products is needed (ncluding also returns, day passes, carnets, season passes, etc.) as well their particular restriction on use (peak, off-peak, eligibility, etc), all described in a machine-readable representation. Computation of the ‘best’ fare may thus also require additional consideration of the frequency and time of travel, the type of user and their eligibility for discounts, etc.Answering the “best value” question is therefore a much more ambitious target for data suppliers. The ‘basic’ fare profile is focused primarily on the first case.6.3Work flows envisagedXML Documents satisfying the EFIP may be exchanged in many different workflows. In particular, they can be used for arbitrary peer-to-peer exchange as open data sent between any party. The same data may reach a consumer system by multiple routes and still need to be aggregated and integrated unambiguously;120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version thus, it is important that element identifiers are stable and globally unique and that they can be managed through distributed processes rather than a central registrar.Data is likely to be processed according to the following overall scenario (but others are not precluded).1.Fare data will be originated by operators to complement their timetable data. It will reference stop , location  and line data elements already defined and reference by timetables encoded in Netex EPIP .Operators will group route-specific tariffs in a similar manner to the way they group their Timetable data, that is, by line/service. Thus, there will be one or more XML fare documents per timetable, providing the tariffs for the line. This may include both single tripand season pass products.Zonal products that apply to many different lines will be described in one or more separate documents for each operator for a specific network or part of a network. For such products, a definition of the zones will normally exist separately from the products themselves – and may be shared with other operators.For certain products supported by multiple operators there will be a core definition providing a description of the common product and then additional definitions describing the exceptions for specific areas, supplied by each operator. A modular structure is needed to support such a workflow.2.Operators will distribute their data by making it available on their sites, or by sending to one or more preferred aggregators.3.Price data can be stated separately from the definition of the products (even if they are included in the same document for the purposes of distribution); typically, the networks and products themselves are fairly static over time; prices may change more often.A common workflow is for one party to act as an aggregator, collecting data from multiple stakeholders andvalidating and integrating it into a useful data set for other consumers. In practice, a workflow that allows peer-to-peer exchange can equally well be used for aggregation.Data will include information as to data source, validity etc that can be used to manage documents.Certain common definitions will apply to all EFIP fare data. These definitions can be provided as part of the common fare profile (versioned so as to allow for future evolution). 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 6.6.8Relating tariffs to the networkA fundamental goal of the fare profile is to ensure that the data allows tariffs to be related to the underlying network by journey planners and fare calculation engines, so that appropriate products and prices can be found for trips.Although it is straightforward in NeTEx to specify that specific tariffs or fare products apply to specific timetabled journeys, this is not the general case; rather the tariff is normally stated in terms of what parts of the network (lines, operators, zones, stops etc) and when (time bands etc) it applies to and a trip planneror fare engine must use the trip parameters to select the available fares.Different methods are used to relate different spatial tariff structures to the network:For point-to-point tariff between stops, the origin and destination stops can be related directly to the DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT linking the stops using their stop identifiers.For a flat fare for a single line, the journey pattern can be used to determine all of the stops of the line.For a zonal tariff, stops can be assigned to a tariff zone; a trip planner first determines fares that apply between the origin and destination zones. In principle, different methods might be used to make the assignment of stops to zones:oExplicitly: i.e. membership of a stop of a tariff zone is stated by a link.oImplicitly by projection: thus, if the coordinates of the stop fall within the bounding polygon of the containing tariff zone, it is deemed to be within the zone. This however cannot be done oBy boundary stop: the stops on the boundary are enumerated and all stops enclosed in thepolygon are included.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Figure 11 – Relationship of stops to zonesThe data model specifies which stops are in which tariff zone: a stop may be in more than one zone,zones maybe specific to an operator or shared with other operators.Both stops and zones have a spatial projection (i.e. spatial coordinates). However spatial containment of a stop within a tariff zone’s extent does not invariably imply semantic membership of the zone. Therefore, although in many cases the stop coordinates can be used to compute whichstops are in a given tariff zone so as to populate the membership links of the tariff zone, it is nonetheless important to have an explicit representation.EFIPEFIPEFIP assumes membership is explicitly stated. it is of course possible to use a process to populate a set of explicit memberships from spatial data.6.6.8.1Who is responsible for defining tariff zones?For zone-based tariffs, a key question is who is responsible for defining the tariff zones, that is, for allocating to them identifiers and names and specifying which stops and lines are included? Tariff zones, like stops, need to have unique persistent identifiers.Responsibility may be specific to an operator or authority, or be managed by a wider consortium.Responsibility typeDescriptionExample120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

National Rail Network areasCommon organisation e.g. DiscoveryScheme Defined Zones are defined by an organisation other than an operator E.g. National Concession SchemeOther shared zonesZones are defined by a specific operator and are shared with others.Operator specificZones are defined by a specific operator and are specific to that operator.E.g. EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version In transitProduct is bought onboard during travel or at exit point; effectively the same as prepaid except that it is permitted to start travel without a ticket. Extended profilePay as you go at start of travelThe user is charged on starting the journey at check in. Extended profilePay as you go Prepayment with adjustment The user is charged on starting the journey, either for the correct amount, or for an average fare. On completion of the journey, any difference from actual rights consumed is used to make an adjustment.The user may be required to keep a credit balance on the card, either manual or using an auto top up. Extended profilePay as you go post paymentThe user is charged on completion of the journey according to the access rights consumed (by explicit or implied check out). Fare calculation may include capping or usage-based rebates.e.g. Tfl Oyster, Tfl Oyster Contactless.Extended profilePrepaid with rebate.Account based and smart card systems may offer the user rebates if they exceed certain usage thresholds.FutureTable 15 – Common Product Charging momentsIn NeTEx, a NeTEx CHARGING MOMENT is used to represent the time of payment (pre-/post- payment) and the account location (card, backoffice).6.6.11Commonly found Fare Demand typesSome tariffs are often restricted to certain periods so as to spread demand on congested networks. A different tariff may apply to night operation. ABO products.Weekends and holidaysTariff may be used only at weekends and on public holidaysNightTariff applies only at evenings or at night; Table 16 – Common Time demand typesIn NeTEx, a FareTimeDemandType is used to represent a demand period. The actual days and hoursof operation can be specified with DAY TYPEs6.6.12Temporal factors for peak and off-peak demandThis section presents visualisations of temporal concepts concerning time demand periods – in order to spread demand on the network to less congested periods, differential pricing is common for peak and off-peak periods.Blackout periodsFigure 12 shows that a travel period may be banded so as to create peak and off-peak periods that meet various conditionsDay Type. Specifying the type of day (week day, holiday, etc.) under which the condition applies.Timeband: specifying a period within the day.Time at stop. A further complication is that the time-band may be directional (e.g. into or out of a specific station) and that the specific time may vary station by station, so for example the time band starts at 08:50 at station A, 08:53 at Station B, 08.58 at station C, etc.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Figure 13 – Time demand restrictions on an individual Trip6.6.13Single and multiple operator accessA tariff may be specific to an operator, such that it can only be used on that operator’s services, or be honoured by multiple operators (for example the UK common zonal addon for rail, PlusBus). Or it may be that different operators offer different tariffs covering overlapping areas, or specifically cover journeys that go from one operator’s services to another’s.In NeTEx access rights and tariffs may be specific to an OPERATOR or to a GROUP OF OPERATORs, The OPERATOR and GROUP usually, but not always, sell their own tickets. Other distributors may also be used, such as TRAVEL AGENTS, or o a RETAIL CONSORTIUM (a sales organisation representing a group of operators).6.6.14Commonly found Passenger Types in EFIPTariffs usually segment the market, with different prices for different types of users. Certain products or certain discounts to products may be restricted to specific types of user who must meet specified eligibility criteria.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Some of these categories may be immediately available to anyone (e.g. Adult, child), others may require documentary proof (e.g. a disabled card) and/or prior registration (e.g. to get a senior discount in the TfL you must have a Senior Oyster card).Some profiles entitle the user to take companions (children, assistants, dogs, etc.) either free or at a discount (so can be considered as “group tickets”).Certain user profiles may be ordained by statutory measures, with eligible users being allowed a concessionary discount.User profiles may also apply to the purchase of group tickets – see next section.Commonly found user profiles in many countries are shown in Table 17Table 17. Although broadly the same categories are found in most countries, for the age dependent user types, there are distinct variationsas to the ages for eligibility in specific groups.NatureDescriptionAdultAny person too old for a child or youth discount. Some products may have a minimum age. E.g. in order to gain the right to take an infant free or a child at a discount.Infant Babes in arms and children. Maximum age varies between countries – from 4 to 6 years old. Usually travel free without a seat. Cannot travel unaccompanied.Child (Accompanied)Children who are no longer infants, accompanied by a parent or sibling.Usually discounted by between 30-50%, or free.Both the minimum and maximum ages for being eligible for child fares vary considerably between countries an even within countries. Minimum is typically between 5 and 7 years old, maximum varies from 12 to 16 years old. In a few cases there is even variation within a country for specific products (for example, some airport shuttle services).Some countries / products require the child to be a descendant (i.e. son/daughter or grandchild of the adult).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Child (Unaccompanied)A child (see above) travelling without an adult. Usually eligible for a discounted fare, but not free as for some fares for accompanied children.There is usually a minimum age for being allowed to travel unaccompanied. YouthYoung person over the minimum age for child discounts (see above - typically 15 years) and below the age for adult (e.g. between 18 and 26 years).PupilSchool children in full time education. Usually under 19.Student / TraineePerson attending an accredited college. Usually under a certain age (e.g. 26), with special provision for PhDs and mature students.SeniorPersons over a certain age (or formally registered as a pensioner), oftenstatutory. Usually eligible for a discount. Age varies between countries (typically between 60 and 65 years old, but in some cases as high as 70). Some rights may be restricted to local residents or nationals. A fewcountries have different age bands for seniors (e.g. 0-65, 65-70, 70+) with different rights. DisabledUsually Statutory. May be subdivided into different categories, e.g., Blind, severely handicapped, handicapped child, invalid, etc.Disabled CompanionCompanions to disabled, veterans or other specific types of user. Usually discounted or free.Social BenefitsUnemployed, qualifying for special social benefits, etc. Usually registered membership scheme.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

Most supplements apply to long distance travel.6.7Overview of other EFIP product featuresIn addition to the tariff aspects discussed above (temporal and spatial, user type etc)EFIP Tariffs may be based on a number of other factors such as the ticket media, distribution channel, etc., etc.), as described below.Each of these aspects correspond to specific components in the NeTEx Model, some relevant components are noted in each section so as to give an indication as to how the fare might be encoded as a NeTEx document.6.7.1Commonly found Types of Travel Document in EFIPIn order to travel, passengers require some form of travel document to prove they have purchased the required access rights. Travel documents may take the form of either paper, plastic or electronic media. Foreither commercial or technical reasons, certain products may be available only on certain media. Electronic and Account Based Ticketing fares are also becoming increasingly common, in some systems a payment card or device may be used as a travel document.Since there are different levels of operational cost and convenience associated with different media types, sales offer packages may be priced differently according to the media they use.Type of Travel DocumentNameNotePaper TicketPrinted or handwritten paper ticket. EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version TripSingleCan modify time of travel/ ExchangeWhether the time of travel or route can be modified.EXCHANGING usage parameter.Can RefundWhether the user can obtain a refund for and unused or partly used product, either ahead of travel or after having failed to use it.Mainly relevant for season passes.REFUNDING usage parameter.Replacement possibleWhether a replacement can be obtained if lost, possibly retaining the balance on an account-based product.Mainly relevant for season passes and smartcard productsREPLACING usageparameter.Table 25 – Commonly found After Sales ConditionsIn NeTEx, a number of different types of USAGE PARAMETER are used to set additional commercial conditions on post-sales handling of the ticket. These can include EXCHANGING, REFUNDING, REPLACING, etc., etc.6.8Summary of EFIP Price featuresAn important aspect of fares is of course the fare price, that is, the monetary amount associated with each selectable combination of tariff features.In NeTEx, prices are represented separately from the elements that are priced, making it possible to have successive prices sets at different times with different validity periods, without altering the tariff structure. It also allows for prices for the same products to be stated in different currencies, or even, for example, be stated in other valuation units such as frequent flier points from a membership scheme.Prices are represented as specialisations of FARE PRICE.Sets of prices can be organised using a FARE TABLE – see later below. Prices may be included in the same document as the fare structure, or be exchanged separately.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version It is also possible to indicate that some or all prices are supplied dynamically through a pricing service, as for many yield managed products.6.8.1Pricing dimensionsAnything which is a pricing factor for a EFIP Fare needs to be represented by a priceable model element in the profile. Thus, for example for a point to point fare where ther is a separate price for each origin/destination pair, there should be a price for each  DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT.   Other triff elementscommonly  requiring a price are  TARIFF ZONEs, TIME INTERVALs,HEOGRAPHIC INTERVALs  USER PROFILEs, TYPES OF TRAVEL DOCUMENT all variously contribute to prices and so are included in the profile.Other factors may be relevant for passenger information, but do not necessarily need to be modelled for  pricing. For example, if different prices are offered for children and seniors for the same product , then a user type (USER PROFILEs - e.g. adult, child, senior) needs to be in the profile and to have an associated price. But  if the product is marketed as two separate SALES OFFER PACKAGEs, the different prices can be associated with the SALES OFFER PACKAGE and not with the USER PROFILE i.The price table for a given SALE OFFER PACKAGE is typically an NxN matrix of all the relevant factors, (possibly with certain combination instances excluded.). The actual choice of factors depends on the tariff structure and product type..120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version  Figure 14 – Pricing dimensions.The most common combinations of tariff factors affecting prices  found in price tables are the following:6.8.1.1Point-to-point tripDISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT x USER PROFILE x (SALES OFFER PACKAGE)6.8.1.2Stage fare tripGEOGRAPHICAL INTERVAL x USER PROFILE x (SALES OFFER PACKAGE)6.8.1.3Zonal FareFARE ZONE x TIME INTERVAL x USER PROFILE x (SALES OFFER PACKAGE)Where the SALES OFFER PACKAGE also embodies a specific media (TYPE OF TRAVEL DOCUMENT) – such as paper, smartcard, or mobile app – and DISTRIBUTION CHANNEL (e.g. at stop, on-board, online).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 6.8.2Other pricing dimensions Other factors may also apply, for example the FULFILMENT METHOD, if an additional charge is charged for delivery by post or courier.The following figure summarises the NeTEx elements most relevant as price dimensions.Figure 15 – NeTEx Pricing dimension elements6.8.2.1.1Associating fares with pricesNeTEx allows sevel different ways of attaching prices to PRICEABLE OBJECTs.  For simplicity the EFIP uses FARE TABLES and CELLS.   Each CELL references all the factors contributing to the CELL PRICE.Note: NeTEx also supports the use of specific prices,  DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT PRICEs, USAGE PARAMETER PRICEs etc.6.8.3Absolute and derived pricesPassenger information for EFIP fares includes both prices stated as absolute amounts (e.g. €4.50) and pricesstated as a derivation of another price (E.g. The child price is 50% of the adult price), or each additional person on a group ticket is €1.00).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version NeTEx supports complex price derivation rules, with discounting factors and minimum and maximum limits, so that one price may be derived from another (using a PRICE RULE, DISCOUNTING RULE or LIMITING RULE).ROUNDING rules may be specified for the calculation so that the result is quantized to specified currency units.The derivation of a price may be recorded as a RULE STEP.Prices may be attached to many different types of tariff structure and fare product element. The allowed combinations of prices of the different types of factor can be used to create an n-dimensional pricing matrix.Rules may be chained, so for example one might apply successive discounts and then calculate added tax.6.8.4Dynamic pricesIn NeTEx, prices can also be dynamic (rather than static), that is, fetched from a named pricing service at the time a fare offer is made. Dynamic pricing is mainly used for yield managed fares on long distance travel, i.e. not on local buses.6.8.5Prices in the EFIPBasicFIn the EFIP Basic Fare profile all prices should be absolute – derived prices should not be used.ExtendedFIn the EFIP Extended Fare profile derived prices are allowed. Derived prices are specified usingPRICING RULES, and a base price. ROUNDING parameters may also be specified.6.8.6Indirection -Use of Price groupsIt is common practice to simplify tariffs by grouping prices into price bands (For example Band A = €1.00, Band B=€1.50, Band C = €1.80. etc). Sometimes these will be used as an internal mechanism that is not exposed to passengers, in other cases they will be shown on published tariffs (e.g. see Figure 16).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version  Figure 16 – Example of use of price groups in a tariff (Adria Mestre)The PRICE GROUP element can be used in NeTEx to represent a shared price.If the price bands are based on a number or range of units, a GEOGRAPHICAL INTERVAL PRICE can be used directly as a “price band”6.8.7Organisation of EFIP pricesThe exchange of fare data typically involves the exchange of large numbers of prices. Typically, there will bea separate price for each permitted combination of the discrete tariffs and fare product elements.NeTEx provides a structure, the FARE TABLE to allow the efficient organisation of prices as nested tables. A fare table is made up of CELLs.Prices can be arranged as hierarchically nested rows, with tariff elements attached to each nesting level, as shown in Figure 17.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

necessarily expose the underlying fare structure use by the operator – for example:What are actually kilometre distance-based fares may be translated into simple point-to-point fares.Price groups may be used to give the same fare to many different journeys, but users will not necessarily be aware of them as they will only see the resolved prices assigned to each individual trip.Separately priced product elements (such as parking, a trip and travel in a destination zone) may becompounded into a single sales offer so that the underlying discrete pricing of elements is not visible.6.8.10Other factors used in Tariff StructuresThere may be other factors relevant for tariff structures, either limiting tariffs more specifically, or offering a set of prices based on a EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 7EFIP Framework7.1Basic elementsThe Transmodel NeTEx fare model used by the EFIP makes use of a number of common NeTEx Framework elements that are described in the NeTEx specifications and in the EPIP, so are not described again here. The framework provides common mechanisms for  all NeTEx objects to support effective data exchange, such as versioning, validity, grouping, and responsibility tracking. The EFIP also makes use of a number of resuable components  suchas calendar, acceesibility etc, etc.   The elements are summarised here – refer tothe other specifcatiosn for further details.7.1.1Core Framework elements (NeTEx Part1)DATA MANAGED OBJECT,VALIDITY CONDITIO,NRESPONSIBILITY SET, ORGANISATION, CONTACT DETAILS, VERSION FRAME,GROUP OF ENTITIES. 7.1.2Generic components (NeTEx Part1)LOCATION, POINT, LINK, ZONE LINK SEQUENCE, PLACE, SECTION, PROJECTION,SECURITY LIST,LOGGABLE OBJECT, EVENT.7.1.3Reuseable Components for Public Transport (NeTEx Part1)ACCESSIBILITY ASSESSMENT,FACILITY SET, FACILITY,TRANSPORT MODE,NOTICE,  ALTERNATIVE TEXT,SERVICE CALENDAR, DAY TYPE, PROPERTY OF DAY, OPERATING DAY, DAY TYPE ASSIGNMENT.7.1.4Network elements also used in EPIP (NeTEx Part1/Transmodel Part 1&2)The Transmodel/NeTEx fare model used by the EFIP also makes use of the same descriptive elements that are used to describe the network topology (as stops, lines, zones etc). 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version The coloured data elements in the figure below show the concrete entities (abstract classes are greyed).Figure 19 - High level Overview of Network  Model (UML)Network elements  includeLINE, GROUP OF LINES, NETWORKOPERATOR, AUTHORITY, GROUP OF OPERATORSVEHICLE MODESTOP PLACE, SCHEDULED STOP POINT and STOP ASSIGNMENTSee EPIP for further details.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 7.1.5Timetable  elements also used in EPIP (NeTEx Part2/Transmodel Part 3)The Transmodel / NeTEx fare model used by the EFIP also makes use of some of the same descriptive elements that are used to describe the  timetables ( service journeys,  etc). These includeSERVICE JORUNEY, TRAIN NUMBERee EPIP for further details.8EFIP:  OverviewThe remaining parts of this document give a summary of the elements in the EFIP fare profile. Extracts of Transmodel / NeTEx data models show in colours concrete data elements (abstract classes are greyed).8.1Fare Specification aspectsNeTEx defines fare structures and prices using a layered set of reusable components. The following diagramsummarises the main layers as four separate columns : Sales Offer, Fare Product, Fare Structure and Fare Price (visualised from left to right).A TARIFF is a coherent set of FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs which serve to describe the differentaspects of the fare structure and relate them to the underlying transport network - for example theavailable tariff zones, stop-to-stop transitions, pass durations, etc.A FARE PRODUCT is a marketable set of access rights, specified as one or more VALIDABLEELEMENTs, each referencing one or more FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENT.A SALES OFFER PACKAGE packages one or more FARE PRODUCTs as an offering which may bepurchased by a customer; it adds information of the types of ticket and distribution channels andother commercial conditions. It also specifies what TYPE OF TRAVEL DOCUMENT (i.e. how theproduct is materialised as a ticket).FARE PRICEs specify the prices for the different elements to which a price may attach. Prices maybe held at many different levels and there will be separate prices for each allowed combination ofproduct options.Each of these aspects is described further in the chapters later below.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Figure 20 - High level Overview of Network  Model (UML)8.2Fare Model SubmodelsNeTEx Fare model is modularised into a number of submodels defined as UML packages, these in turndepend on packages in NeTEx Part1 and Part2 that define framework and transport network components.The FARE ZONE Models describe the network related fare constructs.The FARE PRICE Model present FARE PRICEs and PRICE GROUPs; the FARE CALCULATION Modelholds common pricing parameters; The FARE TABLE Model presents FARE TABLEs for organisinglarge groups of prices.The FARE STRUCTURE Models provide the various types of element used to represent differenttariff structures.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version The FARE PRODUCT Models describes the available FARE PRODUCTs.The USAGE PARAMETER Models describe the limiting conditions for the fare products.The FARE ACCESS RIGHT PARAMETER Models assign the access rights to specific products andlimiting parameters.The SALE OFFER PACKAGE Model describes how the fare elements are combined as marketablecomponents using various TYPES OF TRAVEL DOCUMENT The FARE FRAME Model describes the elements used to group fare data for exchange.9EFIP:Fare Network  9.1GeneralNeTEx Part1 includes the concept of a TARIFF ZONE, which can be used to define the permanent fare zonesof a network. A given SCHEDULED STOP POINT can belong to one or more TARIFF ZONEs.Many networks will use TARIFF ZONEs. A TARIFF ZONE is a view of a ZONE, specifically defined for fare calculation. It is composed of SCHEDULED STOP POINTs. A TARIFF ZONE may have specific points on its borders, the TARIFF POINTs. Some such points activate an automatic detection of the NeTEx Part3 FARE ZONE MODEL extends these elements and also adds further concepts relating to the network that can be used additionally to underpin fare structures.FARE SCHEDULED STOP POINT specialises SCHEDULED STOP POINT, extending its definition with additional fare related attributesA FARE ZONE is a specialization of TARIFF ZONE that may have additional properties, in particular FARE SECTIONs associated with it.A FARE SECTION is a subdivision of a JOURNEY PATTERN, consisting of consecutive FARE POINTs in PATTERN, each referencing a SCHEDULED STOP POINT in that JOURNEY PATTERN. FARE SECTIONs allowarbitrary sections of the network to be associated with a specific FARE ZONE or staging points.A SERIES CONSTRAINT allows a constraint on a routing between an origin and destination to be   specified, for example that a passenger may travel on any routes that pass through particular intermediate stops, or that TARIFF ZONES must only be traversed in a specifc order. Alternative SERIES CONSTRAINTS can be used to specify different distances for pricing different routings. 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version A SERIES CONSTRAINT allows a routing constraint to be specified concisely as a series of FARE POINTs in JOURNEY PATTERN – series are used in TAP TSI for many rail products. The TAP TSI B1 document defines by a notation for describing the constraints to staff and passengers as a “route description” or itinerary on a ticket.For example: the following set of diagrams show four paths through a simple network from an origin station A to a destination station D, with two designated via stations B and C. Use of the network could be restricted to a specific path, as in (i) [A * B * D] (Figure 22) or (ii) [A * C * D] (Figure 22), or it could allow anypath within an envelope delineated by the intermediate stops, as in [A * (B/C) * D]. In this case, the route description (B/C) does not mean that the passenger must take a train passing exactly by either B or C, it means that the passenger has a choice of all routes between a “tariff envelope” of a station on the left and a station on the right (route range), as shown in (i), (ii), (iii) (Figure 23), and (iv) (Figure 24), . Thus a ticket [A* D] with SERIES CONSTRAINT (B/C) would allow travel along any of the following four routes:Figure 21 — Series Example (i) [A * B * D]Figure 22 — Series Example (ii) [A * C * D ]120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 10EFIP: Pricing10.1GeneralThe FARE PRICE Model allows prices to be specified for any element of the tariff structure which may hold aprice value used in the overall price calculation.An important principle of Transmodel is that prices should be separate from the things that they price. Thisallows for alternative price sets with different validities to be exchanged without revising the rest of thefare model.10.2Fare PricesA FARE PRICE represents an individual price (both value and pricing related parameters) that can be associated with a PRICEABLE OBJECT. Its value is in a stated PRICE UNIT (currency or otherwise).A FARE PRICE may be given as an absolute amount (e.g. €23.00) or be derived using a PRICING RULE from another base price (e.g. the child fare is 50% of adult fare). When it is computed, a derived price is resolved into an absolute value (which may in turn be used as a base price for a further derivation); it may however also retain references to the base price and rule from which it is derived as well as the resulting amount, thus providing a justification.EFIP1FIn the Basic profile all prices are absoluteEFIP2FIn the Advanced profile all prices may also be derivedNote: In NeTEx, FARE PRICEs may also be dynamic (rather than static), that is provided by a named PRICING SERVICE at run time .Transmodel/NeTEx provide two ways of specifying fare pricesAs CELL PRICE fare prices fares associated with a CELL that refernces all the relevant factors (i.e. PRICEABLE OBJECTS, and other elements) for which it represents the specifc priceAs specifc prices directly associated with each different type of PRICEABLE OBJECT that they price.CONTROLLABLE ELEMENT PRICE;FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENT PRICE;GEOGRAPHICAL INTERVAL PRICE;DISTANCE MATRIX PRICE;TIME INTERVAL PRICE;VALIDABLE ELEMENT PRICE;120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version USAGE PARAMETER PRICE;FARE PRODUCT PRICE;SALES OFFER PACKAGE PRICE.FULFILMENT METHOD PRICE.EFIP1FThe EFIP uses CELL PRICEsEFIP1FPRICEs and pricing parameters are placed in a separate frames. Note: In NeTEx, FARE PRICEs may also be dynamic (rather than static), that is provided by a named PRICINGSERVICE at run time. Dynamic prices are excluded from the EFIP.It may be necessary to group price entities into PRICE GROUPs, in order:to group all possible access rights or products into a few categories , each of them having a price reference (products of price ‘A’, ‘B’, etc.); to group prices into categories to which the same increase, in value or percentage, may be applied;To organize large numbers of prices.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

Each price canrepresents a priceable combination that sharesthe common price.«FK»FarePriceRefFarePriceRef+0:1Reference to a FARE PRICE to be used as theprice for the PRICE GROUP.Table 36 – PriceGroup – XML Element10.6Pricing ParametersA set of parameters may be associated with a price to use when computing prices.A PRICING RULE names a method of calculation that a consumer system must understand andinterpret. There are two types of common PRICING RULES that have specific parameters associatedwith them:A DISCOUNTING RULE specifies parameters relating to discounting; discounts may be specified aseither a percentage (e.g. 10%) or an absolute amount (e.g. 5 €).A LIMITING RULE is a DISCOUNTING RULE with further parameters to set limits on the results, forexample to set a minimum and maximum price.More than one PRICING RULE may be applied successively to derive a price; PRICING RULESs may bechained together. (e.g.Adult fare is distance GEOGRAPHIC INTERVAL PRICE à Child fare is 50% of adult fareà Final price is price without tax + Vat at 15%). Where a price is derived from another price, theintermediate derivation steps may be recorded using a RULE STEP RESULT. For example, a concessionaryprice might be calculated from a full fare by applying a discount, then deducting tax.ROUNDING conditions may se specifed for use when computing a derived price.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Table 45 – PricingService – Element10.11 Prices for fare structure elementsAn ENTITY which can have a price is a specialisation of PRICEABLE OBJECT; both individual structuralelements of a TARIFF (DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT, GEOGRAPHCIAL INTERVAL, TIME INTERVAL, FARESTRUCTURE ELEMENT, etc.; product and product offer components (FARE PRODUCT, SALES OFFERPACKAGE, etc); and various other parameters (USAGE PARAMETER FULFILMENT METHOD, etc) arePRICEABLE OBJECTS and may be assigned FARE PRICEs. A pricing algorithm will use these variously tocompute an actual price for a specific set of choices.There are different specialisations of FARE PRICE for different types of PRICEABLE OBJECTs, for example aDISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT PRICE, a FARE PRODUCT PRICE, etc., etc. For brevity, in the EFIP specificationthe separate fare is not used  - CELL PRICEs are used instead.FARE PRICEs can be in any PRICE UNIT (currency or otherwise) and can have a start date.EFIP1>> In the EFIP, all prices must be placed in a separate FARE FRAME formed of the faredescriptions.10.12PriceableObjectAn element which may have a FARE PRICE.Classifi-cationNameTypeCardinalityDescription::>::>DataManagedObject::>PRICEABLE OBJECT inherits from DATAMANAGED OBJECT.«PK»idPriceableObjectIdType1:1Identifier of PRICEABLE OBJECT.NameMultilingualString0:1Name of PRICEABLE OBJECT.DescriptionMultilingualString0:1Description of PRICEABLE OBJECT.Urlxsd:AnyURI0:1URL to web page with information aboutPRICEABLE OBJECT. +v1.1120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version «cntd»infoLinksInfoLink0:*Additional hyperlinks for +v1.1«cntd»alternative-NamesAlternativeName0:*ALTERNATIVE Name for element.«cntd»notice-AssignmentsNotice  Assignment  0:*NOTICE ASSIGNMENTs associated with theelement.«FK»PricingService-RefPricingServiceRef0:1PRICING SERVICE to use to fetch pricesdynamically.«FK»PricingRuleRefPricingRuleRef+0:1Default PRICING RULE to use to derive pricesfrom this element.«cntd»priceGroupsPriceGroup0:*PRICE GROUPs associated with PRICEABLEOBJECT.«cntd»fareTablesFareTable0:*FARE TABLEs associated with PRICEABLEOBJECT.Table 46 – PriceableObject – XML Element10.13Organising prices for exchangeEven a relatively simple tariff structure may have a large number of prices; depending not only on the size of the network, but also combinatorically on the number of different features in the tariff structure. Certaintariff structures, notably point-to-point fares, may have very large price matrices.NeTEx uses FARE TABLEs to organise fares into nested groups so that common properties of prices do not need to be repeated. FARE TABLEs allow any order to be chosen for the hierarchy of nesting used.Each FARE TABLE contains one or more FARE TABLE CELLs. Each cell may hold a FARE PRICE for a specifcPRICEABLE OBJECT. It may also hold reference for one or more PRICEABLE OBJECTs as well as for otherpricing factors that further describe the meaning of the price (see CELL PRICE ASSIGNMENT below). A FARETABLE may include other FARE TABLEs (but not include itself, either directly or indirectly).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage  of Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu
