EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 15Common rules for implementing the EFIP15.1Rules for Simplifying  EU Fares ProfileThis section sets out common rules for the fare profile (EFIP), to make the fare offer description as simple and as systematic as possible: NeTEx very often allows to have different ways of expressing the same information but, in order to ease the production and consumption of data, EFIP adds a set of rumes (some mandatory and some recommended) to avoid having too much diversity in the use of NeTEx across data propviders.Also note that the fare profile follows all the same rules as to versions, identifiers, responsibilities, PublicationDelivery Headers, etc., as in the base EPIP profile – see the EFIP document for details.The EFIP adds several further TYPEs OF FRAME for different types of FareFrame, see below.It also sets out rules for the organisation of data within each FareFrame, see belowTable 131 – Summary of simplification rules of the fare profileCategoryRuleStatusConditions and ParametersSpecify Conditions and Parameters for FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTS where possible (rather than for FARE PRODUCTs/ SALES OFFER PACKAGE)MandatorySpecify conditions and Parameters: at FARE PRODUCT level (via VALIDABLE ELEMENT and FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTS, see rule above), and SALES OFFER PACKAGE level  via FARE TABLE only  MandatoryEmbed VALIDABLE ELEMENTs elements in the FARE PRODUCT that uses themMandatoryAvoid using of complex conditions (AND/OR for the assignment of Recommended120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 284 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version parameters)Limit the organisation of data elements to a uniform approachRecommendedRequire use of TARIFFs to organize access rights and limitations as FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTsMandatoryUse standard categories of TYPE OF FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENT (access, eligibility duration, conditions_of_travel, conditions_of_use) whenever it holds conditions/parameters (and tag the ID accordingly).RecommendedService descriptionMake sure information for a journey planner is there eg ZONEs, SCHEDULED STOP POINTs, etc.RecommendedUse FARE ZONE to specify topology etcMandatoryPricesSeparation of prices – Use of FARE TABLEsMandatoryNest tables to reduce repetitionRecommendedUse precalculated prices where available (instead the PRICING RULE)RecommendedUse PRICING SERVICE for yield managed pricesMandatoryDocument to provide human readable Provide textual Names and Description for productsMandatoryUse TYPE OF FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENT, TYPE OF ASSIGNMENT to set explicit meaningRecommended120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 285 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version descriptionsClassify FARE PRODUCTs and TYPE OF FARE PRODUCTMandatoryInclude SummaryCondition on FARE PRODUCTs and SALES OFFER PACKAGEsMandatoryInclude links back to web pages text and graphicsRecommendedProvide Notices to be printed by tools on the user interfaceRecommended15.2Rules for simplifying use of parameters assignments and conditions Parameter assignments (access right, scoping parameters, validity parameters, etc.) can be defined and assigned a pretty much any level of the fare offer model, according to Transmodel and NeTEx. While this is very flexible, it can quickly become an nightmare at implementation time, especially for the consumer who may face very different data structures depending on the providers, and this without any indication about the implementation choices.Therefore, EFIP defines a set of mandatory rules to make the parameter assignments as systematic as possible. The purpose of these rules is not the optimization nor the strict compliance with the Transmodel rules, but only to facilitate the implementation for data producers and consumers.Rule : Specify Conditions and Parameters within FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs where possible (rather than FARE PRODUCT/SALES OFFER PACKAGE) - MandatoryRule : Specify conditions and Parameters : at FARE PRODUCT level (via VALIDABLE ELEMENT and FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTS, see rule above), and SALES OFFER PACKAGE levEl  via FARE TABLE only  - MandatoryNeTEx allows the the parameter assignments to be hosted in a FareStructureElement, even if this is not themost obvious location for these assignments, it is a very convenient and systematic way to describe them (see the example below). This rule is mandatory.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 286 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version <!--  ==== Conditions of SALE  -================= --><FareStructureElement version="1.0" id="mytrain:my_single_trip@conditions_of_sale"><Name>Conditions of Sale</Name><TypeOfFareStructureElementRef versionRef="efp:v1.0" ref="efp:conditions_of_sale"/><GenericParameterAssignment version="1.0" id=" mytrain:my_single_trip@conditions_of_sale" order="1"><TypeOfAccessRightAssignmentRef versionRef="efp:v1.0" ref="efp:conditions_of_sale"/><limitations><Refunding version="1.0" id=" mytrain:my_single_trip@conditions_of_sale"><Name>NoRefund</Name><Description>Public transport regulations for the Ile-de-France region do not provide for the reimbursement of unused or partially used transportation tickets (purchased individually or in packages of 10)..</Description><Url>https://www.ratp.fr/en/i-have-unused-tickets-can-i-have-them-refunded</Url><Allowed>none</Allowed></Refunding></limitations></GenericParameterAssignment></FareStructureElement>These parameter assignments will then be referred by FARE PRODUCT and their VALIDABLE ELEMENT (notethat another rule is making mandatory to have the ValidableElement embeded inside the FareProduct, and not outside with a reference to it), as described below.<ValidableElement version="1.0" id="mytrain:my_single_trip"><Name>Unlimited rides available for specified duration</Name>120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 287 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version <fareStructureElements><FareStructureElementRef version="1.0" ref="mytrain:my_single_trip@access"/><FareStructureElementRef version="1.0" ref="tsln:my_noctilien@access"/><FareStructureElementRef version="1.0" ref="mytrain:my_single_trip@eligibility"/><FareStructureElementRef version="1.0" ref="mytrain:my_single_trip@conditions_of_sale"/></fareStructureElements></ValidableElement>In some case, parameter assignments have to bas assigned to SALES OFFER PACKAGE. In EFIP this will be done via the FARE TABLE (note that FARE TABLEs are mandatory in EFIP), as described in the example below (this example assignes a sales condition and a price to the SalesOfferPackage).  <Cell version="any" id="lFR-Tarif-Example:Cell:002:LOC"><SalesOfferPackagePrice><Amount>9.00</Amount></SalesOfferPackagePrice><FareStructureElementRef version="1.0" ref="mytrain:my_single_trip@conditions_of_sale"/><SalesOfferPackageRef ref="FR-Tarif-Example:SalesOfferPackage:001:LOC" version="any"/></Cell>As explained above EFIP is making mandatory to have the ValidableElement embeded inside de FareProduct, and not outside with a reference to it.  This is a very simple rule, making data production and consumption more systematic, and corresponding to the fact that in most cases, VALIDABLE ELEMENT are used as a simple link between a FARE PRODUCT and the FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTS (possibly in squence) that it needs to refer. 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 288 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Rule : Embed VALIDABLE ELEMENTs elements in the FARE PRODUCT that uses them- MandatoryAccess right assignment can be very complex (in real life), therefore NeTEx allows complex combination (basically using AND/OR of multiple elements). If this kind of complex construction cannot be fully avoided due to the complexity of some real life fare offers (things like, with some residential eligiblity or a social eligibility, and a employment eligibility in some specific area, on non peak hours and specific working daytype … you are granteed a special fare, but it has some quota limitation and can’t be used more than a certain amount of time ine a day, a week and a month … and no it’s not only imaginitation !), it is strongly recommended to avoid them. Furthermore, since such complex fare will hardly be fully understood by data consumer, keeping a simplistic structured description associated with a fully detailed text NOTICE (via a NOTICE ASSIGNMENT), is most probably the best way to go.Rule : Avoid using complex conditions (AND/OR for the assignment of parameters) - recommendedGrouping FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs  in a TARIFFs as coherent sets which serve to describe the different aspects of the fare structure and relate them to the underlying transport network (for example the available tariff zones, stop-to-stop transitions, pass durations, etc.) is mandatory in EFIP (see 11.2.1 for more details abour TARRIFFs).Rule : Require use of TARIFFs to organize access rights and limitations as FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs – MandatoryAs FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs are also used to host ACCESS RIGHT ASSIGNMENT, giving them an unambiguous type will be a great help for data consumers. EFIP predefines a set of types that can be used directly withouth the need of an explicit type description:access, for access rights (scoping parameters, etc.)eligibility, for eligibility conditionsaccess_duration, for constrints on the duration of the access right120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 289 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version conditions_of_travel,

conditions_of_sale, for conditions lake refund, exchange, etc.Rule : Use standard categories of TYPE OF FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs (access, eligibility, access_duration, conditions_of_travel, conditions_of_use) whenever it holds conditions/parameters - recommendedThese predefined types must be used for TypeOfFareStructureElementRef as below.<TypeOfFareStructureElementRef versionRef="efp:v1.0" ref="efp:access"/>They can also, in addition, be used in IDs with a preceeding “@”as below:<FareStructureElement version="1.0" id="mytrain:my_single_trip@access">Or<GenericParameterAssignment version="1.0" id="mytrain:my_single_trip@access" order="1">15.3Rules regarding the Network StructureEFIP describes the fare offer applicable to a a set of services. Therefore having the fares, without a comprehensive description if the service (timetables, lines, stops, etc.) is useless. In most cases, the service offer will be provided in a separated NeTEx data set (the EPIP Profile should be considered here). NeTEx is offering a nice prerequite mechanism that helps referring other data sets on which the current dataset is relying. In EFIP, it is strongly recommended to make sure that the service offer is fully decribed and properly referenced (including Id and version) with prerequisites.Rule : Make sure information for a journey planner is there eg ZONEs, SCHEDULED STOP POINTs  -RecommendedThese prerequisites can be described as below.<FareFrame version="1.0" id="mytrain:SVC_01@fares"><FrameDefaults>120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 290 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu
conditions_of_use , for usage parameters (companion, user profile,etc.)

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version …</FrameDefaults><prerequisites><InfrastructureFrameRef version="1.0" ref="mytrain:SVC_01@infrastructure"/></prerequisites>The service offer can contain TARIFF ZONEs (see EPIP). However, at the fare level, EFIP makes mandatory touse the FARE ZONE, that must be build from the TARIFF ZONE, enhancing it with additional infomration like the zone topology, it’s scoping method (is it built from its boundaries, or from the contained stops), or it’s neigbours and contained areas. 15.4Rule : Use FARE ZONE to specify topology etc  - MandatorRules regarding the PricesEFIP make mandatory to systematically present the prices through a FARE TABLE. The objective here is to systematize the production and interpretation of fare data, but also to avoid too great a disparity in the choices and modeling options that would inevitably arise (especially in the fare domain) if they were not somewhat constrained.The presentation of prices in the form of a fare table remains a great classic in the field of transport, and this choice will also allow the simplification of the presentation of the offer to the traveler (which is consistent with the profile's objective of providing traveler information).In addition, there are cases where an additional price information may be provided outside of FARE TABLEs (it is a complement, not a replacement): for example, if a product offers a fixed reduction, for example of €5, this may be explicitly indicated in the description of the fare product so as to be able to give a complete and relevant description to the traveler.EFIP chooses not to provide cumulative discounts (is this really happens, they have to be grouped in a single“coumpound” discount).A FARE TABLE allows the representation of price groups for combinations of fare elements. It defines a multidimensional matrix of CELLS, each of which can indicate a price for a combination of one or more fare 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 291 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version elements. For example, we can have USER PROFILE + DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT + CLASS OF USE references on each cell in order to define adult and child fares for first and second class.The tables can be nested (allowing to defined reusable tables that can be included by several others); All objects inheriting from "PRICEABLE OBJECT" (which is the case for the majority of objects in this profile) canbe used within a FARE TABLE.The FARE TABLE allows to define a whole set of relatively independent and effectively reusable componentsthat will be assembled (by reference) within the table to provide a corresponding price (for example: Ticket from A to B + single ticket + young traveler (18-25 years old) => Price), therefore keeping a very modular construction.The FARE TABLE makes references to all the elements that constitute it with the exception of the prices themselves which must be completely defined within the FARE TABLE.Rule : Systematically use of FARE TABLEs, including the price information  - MandatoryRule : Nest tables to reduce repetition  - RecommendationRule : Use precalculated prices where available (instead the PRICING RULE) – RecommendationRule : Use PRICING SERVICE for yield managed prices - Mandatory15.5Document to provide human readable descriptionsIt is fundamental that the fare offer description contains a comprehensive description in order to provide a useful information when displayed to end users. But this is also important for data consumer to be confident on the fact that some data elements will be provided as expected. Also they are not about the data structure itself, the following rules are highly important.Rule : Provide textual names for FARE PRODUCTs  - Mandatory120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 292 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version <InfoLink>https://www.iledefrance-mobilites.fr/en/the-network/mobility-services/bus/noctilien-night-buses</InfoLink></infoLinks>Rule : Provide Notices to be printed by tools on the user interface - RecommendationNotices is very important to add an explanation for the end users. It is highly recommended that the providers include such notices in their datasets, and it is mandatory for consumer to display them the the end users.Another important element in the context of EFIP is that the Notices shall be exclusively attached (assigned)to a FARE TABLE (FareTable), which will in fact allow it to be associated with a SALES OFFER PACKAGE (SalesOfferPackage), or possibly with a FARE PRODUCT (PreassignedFareProduct). Theoretically, a note canbe attached to any object, but attaching notes without a rule to many places would make the exploitation of the data very delicate.It will also often be useful to offer translations of the notices in different languages.15.6Allow simplified fare offer descriptionAs explained above Notices are an important element in communicating about pricing (have to look at a price sheet to see that it is riddled with asterisks and references to a whole series of notices). It should also be noted that, the implementation of EFIP may be more delicate than the other profiles (EPIP,EPIAP) because there are very few fare offers already fully described in a structured and digital way (still mostly web pages and PDF documents to present the fare offer): the initial structured representation work will therefore be relatively long and substantial and it will be useful to proceed in stages. One solution to proceed step by step could be to start by structuring only the key elements of products: describe the fundamentals (FareStructureElement as the origin-destinations or a timing structures for example), the fare product (PreassignedFareProduct) and the sales offer (SalesOfferPackage) but with very120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 296 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version simple parameters (ValidityParameter, UsageParameter, etc.), and assemble the whole in a FareTable in which are added notices specifying the rights. The idea here is typically to provide enough elements so that an information system (trip calculator, service in a MaaS context, etc.) can offer the fare offer description, when it has a good chance of being relevant and that the associated notices allow the user to decide whether the ticket suits them or not. The figure below shows a TER (Bourgogne-Franche-Compté) ticket that can be described in detail with NeTEx but which requires a number of combined user profile elements (employee of companies, administrative agent, other professionals, etc.), which will also require a registration of the professional or his company, etc. These access rights and user profile elements can, as a first approach, be inserted in a note: by reusing the “standard” title (PreassignedFareProduct), a dedicated sales offer (SalesOfferPackage)will be created that will be associated, in the FareTable, with a note (Notice) and information on the corresponding reduction (via a FarePrice), here 30%.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 297 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Figure 60 — Example of a special rate that can justify the use of a notice to simplify it15.7Guidelines for presenting EFIP data in tables and spreadsheetsIt is often useful to have fare price data available as a spreadsheet or other simple table format. It is straightforward to establish mappings between the EFIP core fare elements and such tables and generic principles can be used to identify data elements and to combine normalised model elements in a smaller number of tables as show below.The qualified name of the NeTEx schema attribute shall be used as a column heading for a given attribute ina table. Columns may include both values, references to elements in other tables (since NeTEx uses a unified system of identifiers, codespaces and references) and “view” elements, derived from other tables through the reference, that annotate or explain the references o that data in a given table can be understood. Each table shall have a default codespace: data identifiers and references in the table that are not in the default codespace shall be qualified with the codespace prefix.As well as a table of prices, tables to describe the tariff components making up the prices in the table can also be defined, for example, TARIFF ZONES, GEOGRAPHIC INTERVALS, TIME INTERVALS, FARE PRODUCTS, SALES PACKAGES, etc. Commercial conditions not relevant for access or pricing are omitted in the example (e.g. USAGE PARAMETERS for EXCHANGING. REFUNDING, etc) but could be added.The essential data for many basic  fare types can thus be defined by a small number of tables.PricesFARE TABLE – Prices.NetworksFARE ZONEs. Tariff zones – FARE ZONE members – Stops in a zone – 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 299 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version FrameworkCODESPACEsOPERATORSThe rest of this section provides examples of all of the above tables. Only a minimum et of attributes Iincluded.15.8Codespaces and identifiers for fare dataThe use of codespaces and identifiers on fare data is the same as that for timetable data, that is, the fare data itself will generally be assigned to the provider’s ( i.e. operator’s ) codespace, and use their identifier system, but will by preference use reference data from national data sets such as operator codes ( ), stops ( ) places ( ) and   tariff zones ( ).The identifiers of fare data elements must be stable and unique within their codespace.The identifiers for tariff zones may in particular need to be shared by many operators; a single organisation should be assigned responsibility for issuing the identifiers for a Network.  Note: a recommended convention for naming DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENTS is to use a ‘+‘ to connect the identifiers of the origin and destination zone or stops. For example<DistanceMatrixElement version="1.0" id="myb:4400CY0037+4400CY0038"><Name>Alpha+Beta</Name><StartStopPointRef version="naptStop:any" ref="naptStop:4400CY0037"/><EndStopPointRef version="naptStop:any" ref="naptStop:4400CY0038"/></DistanceMatrixElement><DistanceMatrixElement version="1.0" id="myb:Zone1+Zone3>120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 301 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version <Name>Alpha+Beta</Name><StartTariffZoneRef version="any" ref="myb:Zone1"/><EndTariffZoneRef version="any" ref="myb:Zone3"/></DistanceMatrixElement>15.9Identifiers for version frames containing fare dataExactly the same principles may be used for the identifiers of version frames containing fare data as those described for other types of data in the profile – see the EPIP for details..To recap -The structure of a EFIP VERSION FRAME identifier is:[country-code] : [local-code] : [NeTEx-frame-type] _ [EFIP-type] : [frame-topic]15.10Names for documents containing fare dataExactly the same principles may be used for the names of documents containing fare data as those described for other types of data in the EFIP- in the profile – see the EPIP sspecification for details.Thus: The structure of the file name for a EFIP conformant document is:[prefix] - [EFIP-version] _ [country-code] _ [provider-code] _ [profile-type] _ [doc-topic] _[ creation-date]Example file names:FX-PI-01_FR_METR_LINE-FARE _L01_20160302.xmlFX-PI-01_FR_FEBR_NETWORK-FARE_WOE-060+086+110_20230909.xmlFX-PI-01_FR_DFT_STOP-FARE_NPTG-Plusbus-UK_20150705.xml15.11Version management of elementsThe versioning and validity condition management in the context of the European passenger information profile is simple and mainly based on the FRAME concept.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 302 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version The main rules for object versioning are the following:All objects having an Id shall also have a version (if the version is not managed or unknown, fillthe version information with "any");Also note that the Id shall be stable: an update of an object shall not change the Id;As required by FRAMEs, all the versions of objects within a FRAME shall be consistent all together (but their version value can be decided on a per object base);The versions of the FRAMEs are mandatory and must be :A number (sequence of digits)Any FRAME version provided after another one (update of a FRAME with the same Id) shall have a version number higher than the previous one.The main rules for VALIDITY CONDITIONs are the following:The main VALIDITY CONDITION is the one of the FRAME: any VALIDITY CONDITION of an object being out of the VALIDITY CONDITION of the FRAME will not be taken into account;If a version of a FRAME has part of all its VALIDITY CONDITION overlapping the VALIDITY CONDITION of a previous version of the same FRAME (same Id), the new version completely overrides the previous one in the overlapping period (therefore cancelling all the previous information and replacing them with the new ones)A FRAME shall have VALIDITY CONDITIONsVALIDITY CONDITION of COMPOSITE FRAME is acting on the contained FRAME as the FRAME'sVALIDITY CONDITION is acting on the contained objects.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 303 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 15.12Versions of the profileThe EFIP itself is given a version number to  allow implementors to determine the version  of the profile being used (as opposed to the version of the NeTEx schema)The profile's version codification is the following:for PublicationDelivery: x.y:EU-FP-a.0 (e.g. "2.0:EU_FP-1.0").for FRAME (via TypeOfFrame): x.y:EU_FP_nnnn-a.b (e.g. "2.0: EU_FP_COMMON-1.0").The codification components are the following:1)x.y is the NeTEx version (mandatory, it is the XSD version)2)":" is a mandatory delimiter3)EU_FP (European Passenger Fare Profiley) and EU_FP_nnnn where nnnn is "EU_FP_COMMON","EU_FP_STOP”, "EU_FP_NETWORK", "EU_FP_TIMETABLE", " EU_FP_CALENDAR" or"EU_FP_LINE_OFFER" for TYPE OF FRAME1)"-" is a mandatory delimiter2)a.b is the profile version (mandatory). a and b are integer a=main version b=secondary version number (always 0 in PublicationDelivery)EXEMPLE<PublicationDelivery version="2.01:EU_FP-1.0" ...<TypeOfFrameRef ref="FR1:TypeOfFrame:NETEX_COMMUN:">version="2.02:EU_FP_LINE_OFFER-1.3"</TypeOfFrameRef>120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 304 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 15.13Structure of IDsWhen exchanging information, it is of high importance to make sure that identifiers are stable and unique. In order to ease interoperability and ensure uniqueness, the European passenger information profile provides some rules to built identifiers. These rules are defined so they can easily be applied on internal identifiers of a system at export time, and processed at import time. They also take advantage of the codespace mechanism proposed by NeTEx.Important Note: identifiers are structured for uniqueness purpose ONLY, there shall never be any attribute extraction from the identifiers.The ID structure of objects is the following: [Country code]:[Local Code]:[object type]:[technical-identifier]:[ID provider for shared IDs] [Country code] is an ISO 3166-1 code of the country (2 characters)[Local Code] is a code uniquely identifying the locality or the provider within the country (maybe a region code like the European NUTS code, an authority code, etc.).The Europen NUTS code is recommended here. However this code is not mandatory if the other elements make the code unique (but surrounding ":" must be present).[object type] is the NeTEx object type (ServiceJourney, PassengerStopAssignment, Line, etc. using exactly the tag format, UpperCamelCase and no space) corresponding to the XML tag, and is provided to avoid any collision of single identifiers being used for different types of objects. A small exception is defined for StopPlace in order to differentiate between monomodal and General STOP PLACEs, the [object type] will be MonomodalStopPlace or GeneralStopPlace instead of StopPlace (see Error: Reference source not found-Error: Reference source not found )120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 305 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version [technical-identifier] is a technical identifier for the object, it can be whatever code the system define (built of upper-case or lower case non-accented characters, numbers "-" and "_" ) but shall be unique for the object and durable (a single object can't change its identifier)[ID provider for share IDs] is used to provide a reference to the ID provider (reference data system) when there is one. When this non-mandatory field is present, it ensures by itself the uniqueness and stability of the [technical-identifier].All the ":" separating characters are mandatory (even if a field is empty).Example (purely fictional, just illustrating the coding rule)FR:SMIRT:MonomodalStopPlace:454e74:GE:79189:Line:78-a-1:NO::Route:985a:ENTURFR::MonomodalStopPlace:43289:FR1UK:: GeneralStopPlace:199G98765431:NAPTAN15.13.1Frame IDThe identifier of FRAMEs follows the identifier pattern defined above. However it specialises it in order facilitate the implementation of the rules defined in Error: Reference source not found-120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 306 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Error: Reference source not found and  15.11-Version managementThe FRAME identifier basic structure is:[Country code]:[Local Code]:GeneralFrame/CompositeFrame:EU_FP_nnnn_[VersionNumber]:Where EU_FP_nnnn_[VersionNumber] is:EU_FP_STOP-[VersionNumber] for Stop FRAMEEU_FP_NETWORK-[VersionNumber] for Network FRAMEEU_FP_TIMETABLE-[DaVersionNumberte] for Timetable FRAMEEU_FP_LINE_OFFER-[VersionNumber] for Line FRAMEEU_FP_CALENDAR-[VersionNumber] for Calendar FRAME[VersionNumber] is a number associated with the version of the FrameEXAMPLE : id="FR:RATP:GeneralFrame:EU_FP_LINE_OFFER_20170816103000Z:"15.13.2Other specific coded or IDThe ParticipantRef for National Access Points will simply be the country ISO 3166-1 code.15.14Available types of frameEFP1>>All frames in an EFIP conformant XML document containing fare data must be assigned to oneof the standard EFIP TypeOfFrame types. The available frame types are given below.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 307 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

‘efp:XX_PF_FARE NETWORK’TYPE OF FRAME (FARE FRAME)Data Scope: CONTAINERA CompositeFrame instance conforming to ‘EU_FP_STOP_FARE_OFFER’ shall contain dat about the network for defining fares data for an operator or an area. 15.17Organisation of fare dataThe EFIP sets out a specific structure of organising data within EFIP document in order to facilitate implementation. See also example below.15.17.1Use of framesData should be assigned to one of the specified types of EFIP frame, each classified with a TypeOfFrame, as described above, and indicating the prerequisite frames.1.Basic profile frames as described in Part2 (EPIP) should be used to define any prerequisite dataelements such as OPERATORs, BRANDING and RESPONSIBILITY SETs (in a ResourceFrame of typeEU_FP_COMMON), SCHEDULED STOP POINT, TARIFF ZONEs, etc (in a ServiceFrame of typeEU_FP_NETWORK) etc.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 322 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

efp:EU_FP_NETWORK’TYPE OF FRAME (SERVICE FRAME)1:*

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 2.Fare network data elements, such as a FARE ZONES, FARE SECTIONs, etc (which may be sharedbetween multiple products) should be placed in a separate FareFrame of typeEU_FP_FARE_NETWORK. The frame should declare any prerequisite EU_FP_NETWORK frames uponwhich it depends.3.Elements to define a single product in particular TARIFFs, FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTS,  FAREPRODUCTS, and SALES OFFER PACKAGEs should be placed in a separate FareFrame of typeEU_FP_FARE_PRODUCT. The frame should declare any prerequisite EU_FP_FARE_NETWORK framesupon which it depends.4.Prices for the SALES OFFER PACKAGE should be placed in a separate FareFrame of typeEU_FP_FARE_PRICE; there should be a PRICE FRAME corresponding to each PRODUCT frame givingthe prices for the PRODUCTs. The frame should declare the prerequisite EU_FP_FARE_PRODUCTframe for which it states the prices.15.17.2Organisation of fare product definitionsA EU_FP_FARE_PRODUCT frame should group together the elements needed to define a single fare productor set of closely related products (e.g. a trip, a pass, a carnet that are based on the same tariff structure), along with the SalesOfferPackage instances that define how they are marketed. A product frame should therefore contain.A Tariff, embedding FareStructureElement, DistanceMatrixElement, TimeInterval, GeographicInterval, and FareQualityFactor instances.One or more FareProduct instances:One or more SalesOfferPackage instances, referencing the fare products defined in the frame.There is usually just one Tariff per product, and one or more SalesOfferPackage per fare product.Furthermore:UsageParameter instances should be grouped within FareStructureElement instances in a Tariff(and not be contained directly in the FareFrame). If necessary, a FareStructureElement mayreference a UsageParameter of another Tariff.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 323 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version ValidableElement instances should be nested within a FareProduct (and not be contained directlyin the FareFrame). If necessary, a FareProduct may reference a ValidableElement of anotherFareProduct.Each FareProduct should be categorised with a ProductType and a TariffStructureType.15.17.3Fare table nesting rulesWhen creating FARE TABLEs for the EFIP, the following principles shall be followedg.FARE PRICEs shall be nested in FARE TABLEs for clarity and to reduce data volumes.h.Each NESTING LAYER shall correspond to one further pricing factor i.The type of fare price used shall be for that of lowest level element, For example if the lowest levelis the USER PROFILE (a type of USAGE PARAMETER), such as ‘Adult’ or ‘Child’, then it shall be aUSAGE PARAMETER PRICE, if it is a TYPE OF TRAVEL DOCUMENT (as associated with a SALES OFFERPACKAGE) it shall be a SALES OFFER PACKAGE PRICE, etcj.Where the price is for a specific fare element such as a DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT, TIMEINTERVAL, GEOGRAPHICAL INTERVAL etc, the relevant price shall be embedded in line and mustinclude a reference to the priced element.k.Where the price is for an additional option out of several specified for a single FARE STRUCTUREELEMENT (as say may be the case for a FARE ZONE or a CLASS OF USE, a reference to the specificoption shall be included in the CELL, for example: a FareZoneRef, or ClassOfUseRef, so that aconsuming system can determine its meaning.   l.The preferred order for nesting tables is SALES OFFER PACKAGE / {spatial Factor] / (TIME INTERVAL) / USER PROFILE / CLASS OF USE / Where Spatial factor is either(1)DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT or (2)FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENT + ZONE120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 324 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 15.17.4Nested objects and referencesIt is always preferred to have the description of an object whithin the XML tag hierarchy rather to a reference to an XML object located at the root level of the XML hierarchy. Therefore nested objects shall beused each time it is possible (as opposed to referenced objects). References should only be used when a single object is used several time (to avoid repeating the same information multiple times).This is specifically true for NoticeAssignment (always prefer the Notice to the NoticeRef).15.18Classification of fare elementsData elements should be classified with the NeTEx built in enumerated values and in some cases also the appropriate EFIP TypeOfValue.15.18.1Classifications of TariffsEach EFIP Tariff should be categorised with a TariffBasis and a TypeOfTariff fxc :valueEach EFIP  FareStructureElement should be classified with a TypeOfFareStructureElement efp:valueEach EFIP   AccessRightAssignments should be classified with a TypeOfAccessRightAssignment 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 327 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 15.19Delivery structure (headers)The NeTEx publication schema describes an XML document suitable for exchange either as a stand-alone document, or embedded in a SIRI Delivery (that is the response to a SIRI request for NETEX data). Only the stand-alone document is accepted by the passenger information profile. A publication wraps one or more NeTEx VERSION FRAMEs inside a PublicationDelivery element. Each PublicationDelivery acts as a containerand is annotated with a time stamp.The primary element in the publication schema is a PublicationDelivery, comprising a simple header, followed by a payload of VERSION FRAMES containing NeTEx data objects (limited to the ones selected in the profile: any other NeTEx object remains valid. However it can be safely ignored without breaking the semantic of the dataset). The delivery may also include some optional metadata indicating the classes present in the payload.Table 148 – PublicationDelivery –ElementClassificationNameTypeCardinalityDescriptionversionxsd:NMTOKENVersion identifier of schema. Fixed. PublicationHeaderGroupPublicationTimestampxsd:dateTime1:1Time of output of data.Preferred format UTC. For example,  2017-07-26T13:52:00ZParticipantRefParticipantCodeType1:1Identifier of system providing data. Often this will be the same as the DATA SOURCE but it is possible there may be multiple participant systems belonging to a single data source.Descriptionxsd:normalizedString0:1Description of contents.PayloadGroupdataObjectsdataObjects_RelStructure0:1NeTEx VERSION FRAMES making up publication.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 334 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Delivery example<PublicationDelivery version="2.04:EU_PI-1.0"xsi:schemaLocation="http://www.netex.org.uk/netex NeTEx_publication.xsd"xmlns="http://www.netex.org.uk/netex"xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink"xmlns:ifopt="http://www.ifopt.org.uk/ifopt" xmlns:gml="http://www.opengis.net/gml/3.2"xmlns:core="http://www.govtalk.gov.uk/core" xmlns:siri="http://www.siri.org.uk/siri"xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance">15.20Structure of ReferencesIt is very common in NeTEx to have a reference to another object. Referenced objects can be in the same data set or in another dataset (sent separately). The difference between internal (same dataset) and external object (different datasets) can be made using the version attribute (internal) or versionRef attribute (external). Also note that the use of the version attribute will trigger the NeTEx's XML automatic consistency check (the referenced object must exist in the dataset, if not a validation error is raised). This automatic consistency check will not occur when using versionRef.Table 149 – VersionOfObjectRefStructure –structure120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 335 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Classifi-cationNomTypeCardinalitéDescriptionNameOfRefClassNameOfClass0:1«FK»version VersionRef0:1Reference to version of ENTITY that is in a VERSION FRAME of the current dataset. Mutually exclusive with versionRef.Use "any" if the version is unknown (note that this will trigger the NeTEx's XML automatic consistency check).«FK»refObjectRefObjectIdType1:1«FK»versionRefVersionIdType0:1Identifier of version of  the referenced entity. For use when reference is external (i.e in another dataset) and a specific version is required. Mutuallyexclusive with version.Use "any" if the version is unknown. This will also make clear that the referenced object is external.15.21Empty valuesA lot of NeTEx elements and attributes are not mandatory. This leads to the questions of what should be done with these fields when there is no information to fill them. In XML, three different options are available:1)No XML tag2)Tag with no content <type object></type object> 3)Empty tag <type object/>120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 336 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version For the European passenger information profile, the first option (no XML tag) is the preferred one, and the second one is not accepted.16Example XML: Code organisation for a simple fare product.The following example encodes a simple point-to-point tariff for a line with just three stops, Alpha, Beta and Gamma, along with a fare product, scales offer package and prices. EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version <!--==== Fare Product ==== --><fareProducts><PreassignedFareProduct version="1.0" id="myb:Trip@single">::::: FARE PRODUCT DEFINITIONs</PreassignedFareProduct><!--==== Other Products ==== --></fareProducts><!--==== Sales Packages==== --><salesOfferPackages><SalesOfferPackage version="1.0" id="myb:Trip@single-SOP@p-ticket">::::: SALES OFFFPACKAGE DEFINITIONs</SalesOfferPackage></salesOfferPackages></FareFrame>16.1.4.1Example XML: Tariff in a EU_FP_PRODUCT frameThe following XML fragment shows a definition of a TARIFF for the Mybus Line3 example.The TARIFF defines three separate  FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs. Each is used to specify a independent aspects of the    fare structure a)Access rights (classified as ‘efp:access’ with a TypeOfFareStructureElementRef element), definedwith  a list of DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENTs, one for each possible stop combination.  This isspecifying which network access rights may be consumed. Since this is a point to point fare, a list ofDISTANCE MATRX  ELEMENTs  is given, each specify an origibn/ destination pair. When the userbuys the productt,  one of the specified DISTANCE MATRX  ELEMENTs must be selected andindicates the start and end stop for a trip.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 345 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version b)Eligibility  (classified  as ‘efp:eligibility’ with a TypeOfFareStructureElementRef element), definedwith  a list of USER PROFILE usage parameter; in this case just ‘adult’.  The specified user type canbe validated.c)Travel  (classified  as ‘efp:travel_conditions’ with a TypeOfFareStructureElementRef element),restricted to a single trip with no interchanging  with  a ROUND TRIP and INTERCHANGIING    usageparameters.  The specified travel parametesr indicate how the product can be used are can bevalidated as such defines a VALDIABLE ELEMENT  to specif its ACCESS RIGHTS IN PRODUCT. The VALIDABLE ELEMENT  references the FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTs defined by the TARIFF on which the product is based. EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version 17Profile compliance and data quality checkingThe checking of profile conformance and of data quality is extremely important for achieving successful interoperability. Data producers need a means to verify that their output is correct and usable. Data consumers need to know that any EFIP compliant document meets a reasonable set of quality criteria before they even attempt to use it. There also needs to be a process for resolving issues in exported data with the producer.See the EPIP specification for a discussion of validation and data quality.17.1Summary of data quality rules for EFIPThe same quality rules apply to the fare profile as to the base profile – See the EPIP specification. In addition, a number of additional semantic rules can be applied that are specific to fare. Some examples are given below.Table 151 – Primary quality checking rulesTypeChecking rule categoryDescriptionDCommon NeTEx semantic rules conformance(General Profile validator)Rules relating to the use of NeTEx elements arising from the real-world semanticsof the objects that they represent; that cannot be or are not expressed in the XML but which apply to any NeTEx schema.The fare structures and product must be a realistic combination, e.g. DayPasswill normally be Zonal, Season tickets, SingleTrip products will be point to point, zonal or stage fares, etc.A TariffZone must have a at least one member ScheduledStopPoint;If the FareZone topology is given as disjoint, then each ScheduledStopPoints should be in only one TariffZoneThere should be a separate SalesOfferPackage for each 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 355 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version TypeOfTravelDocument;Etc.ESpecific profile conformance(EFIPEFP validators)The populating of data values in a profile conformant document shall fully upholdthe specific semantics of the profile:The rules for encoding EFIPEFP identifiers shall be followed.A Tariff must be categorised with a TariffType A FareProduct must be categorised with a ProductType Etc.FData Completeness(EFIPEFP validators)All data required to make the data consistent and usable as a coherent set shall be available. The notion of ‘completeness’ depends on the intended use case; for example, whether all tariff zones or just the tariff zones that are used by a line should be present; or whether only current data is needed or future data as well.Examples for the EFIP include:All the ScheduledStopPoints in a FareZone should be specified as members.A DistanceMatrixElement must be given for each permitted Origin/Destination pair of a point-to-point tariff.All the TariffZone instances must be given for a zonal tariff.A UserProfile must be defined for each type of user.

A FarePrice should be given for every combination of price that is available.Etc.GData PlausibilityValues of parameters should be plausible against common sense measures. (Thus, individual bus trips do not cost £100, tariff distances for buses are not 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 356 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version (General Profile validator)measured in hundreds of miles, child fares are cheaper than adult ones).Prices increase with distance, usually progressively (i.e. more is cheaper)Period pass prices increase with TimeInterval length. (A month cost more than a day).The spatial coordinates of ScheduledStopPoints should lie within those of their TariffZone.TariffZone instances should lie within the bounds of their TopographicPlace.Dates should lie within a few years of the present, durations should be plausible for their usage (e.g. season passes are only available in simple interval multiples of a day, week or month, etc).The public identifiers used to label tariff zones should be unique within their expected scope of use. (For example, many towns and operators will have a ‘Town Centre, but there should only be one ‘Town Centre (at least per Operator) in a given town).Instances of PriceRule, Roundings, etc referenced other elements and needed to calculate prices should be defined. HData Relevance(Profile validator)Documents should not contain irrelevant or unused instances of data. (this requires a notion of which elements in a frame are the primary payload for the profile). For example, for the FXC fares:Every ScheduledStopPoint present in a document, or one of its components should be used by at least one TariffZone, FarePointInPattern or DistanceMatrixElement.Every TariffZone should be used by at least one DistanceMatrixElement or 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 357 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version FareStructureElement/ GenericParameterAssignment.Etc.IData correctness(Manual inspectionagainst real-world evidence)Data values should accurately describe the real-world entities they are meant to represent.For an EU_FP_NETWORK_FARE_OFFER, all the lines of a Network shall be described.Names of Sales Offer Packages, etc, should be correct and spelled right.For a stage fare, all the Fare Stages for a Line shall be specified.For a zonal fare, all the instances of a TariffZone for a given Network shall be specified.Prices should be correct.Etc.18Data ExchangeIn the context of the European passenger information profile, data exchange will be implemented through XML file exchange. Each file will contain a single PublicationDelivery, itself containing a single CompositeFrame or specific frames RessourceFrame, SiteFrame, ServiceFrame, TimetableFrame,  CalendarFrame (of one of the previously defined types).Three different types of files will be accepted:Line XML files containing a single EU_FP_LINE_OFFER CompositeFrame (single whole line description with stops, topology and timetable).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 358 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Network XML files containing a single EU_FP_NETWORK_OFFER CompositeFrame (set of multiple whole line description).Stop XML files containing a single EU_FP_STOP SiteFrame (comprehensive description of all the stops of a country, an authority or an operator).Note that this file structure result in possible redundancy across files for information like STOP PLACE, CONNECTION, etc. This is, of course, valid and will be managed with a simple rule: the latest version (most recent changed attributes or higher version number when it is a number, if the version is not managed, the last received ones will be used) always wins !18.1File naming The following file name structure is recommended for any file dissemination:Prefix of the file names which have been established and agreed upon by the exchange partners, here NETEX_FPMajor version of the profile; currently 01country ISO code Provider name (use NAP if coming from a national access point)File type (LINE, NETWORK or STOP)For LINE only: line number or name if no number is available (short name, fewer than 15 characters, made only of letters (uppercase and lowercase), numbers and "-") Date of the creation of the data package (YYYMMDD)All fields are separated by "_"Example file name:120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 359 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

Concepts written in uppercase are from the conceptual model (i.e. Transmodel); those in camel-case are specific to the NeTEx physical model. See Transmodel and NeTEx for further information.Basic – In simple  EFIP Further – In enhanced EFIP Additional; - Not in profile (may be used in an extended data set)ACCESS RIGHT PARAMETER ASSIGNMENTBasicThe assignment of a fare collection parameter (referring to geography, time, quality or usage) to an element of a fare system (access right, validated access, control mean, etc.).AMOUNT OF PRICE UNITBasicA FARE PRODUCT consisting in a stored value of PRICE UNITs: an amount of money on an electronic purse, amount of units on a value card etc.BLACKLISTADDITIONALA list of identified TRAVEL DOCUMENTs or CONTRACTs the validity of which has been cancelled temporarily or permanently, for a specific reason like loss of the document, technical malfunction, no credit on bank account, offences committed by the customer, etc.BORDER POINTFurtherA POINT on the Network marking a boundary for the fare 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 361 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

May or may not be a SCHEDULED STOP POINT.CANCELLINGFurtherParameter giving conditions for cancelling of a purchased access right.CAPPED DISCOUNT RIGHTFurtherA specialisation of SALE DISCOUNT RIGHT where the discount is expressed as a rule specifying a ceiling for a given time interval. For example, the London Oyster card fare, which charges for each journey until travel equivalent to a day pass has been consumed after which further travel is free at that day.CAPPING RULEFurtherA capping limit for a given time interval, where the capping is expressed by another product. For example, the London Oyster card fare, which charges for each journey until travel equivalent to a day pass for the mode of travel has been consumed.CAPPING RULE PRICEFurtherA set of all possible price features of a CAPPING RULE: default total price, discount in value or percentage etc.CELLBasicAn unique individual combination of features within a FARE TABLE, used to associate a FARE PRICE with a fare element.CHARGING MOMENTBasicA classification of FARE PRODUCTs according to the payment method and the account location: pre-payment with cancellation (throw-away), pre-payment with debit on a value card, pre-payment without consumption registration (pass), post-payment etc.CHARGING POLICYBasicParameter governing minimum amount and credit allowed when consuming a FARE PRODUCT.COMMERCIAL FurtherA category of users depending on their commercial relations with 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 362 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version DISCOUNTING RULEBasicA price calculation rule determined by a set of discounts, depending upon a USAGE PARAMETER, to be applied to a FARE PRICE.DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENTBasicA cell of an origin-destination matrix for TARIFF ZONEs or STOP POINTs, expressing a fare distance for the corresponding trip: valuein km, number of fare units etc.DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENTPRICEBasicA set of all possible price features of a DISTANCE MATRIX ELEMENT:default total price etc.DISTRIBUTION ASSIGNMENTBasicAn assignment of the COUNTRY and/or DISTRIBUTION CHANNEL through which a product may or may not be distributed.DISTRIBUTION CHANNELBasicA type of outlet for selling of a product.ELIGIBILITY CHANGE POLICYFurtherParameter indicating the action to be taken when a user’s eligibilitystatus changes.ENTITLEMENT CONSTRAINTFurtherConstraints on choices for an dependent entitled product relative to the required choices for the prerequisite entitling product.ENTITLEMENT GIVENFurtherParameter indicating whether a particular FARE PRODUCT provides an entitlement to buy or use an access right.ENTITLEMENT PRODUCTADDITIONALA precondition to access a service or to purchase a FARE PRODUCT issued by an organisation that may not be a PT operator (e.g. military card).ENTITLEMENT FurtherParameter indicating whether a particular FARE PRODUCT requires 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 365 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version REQUIREDan entitlement to by or use an access right.EXCHANGINGFurtherWhether and how the access right may be exchanged for another access right.FARE CONTRACTADDITIONALA contract with a particular (but possibly anonymous) customer, ruling the consumption of transport services (and joint services). A FARE CONTRACT may be designed for a fixed SALES OFFER PACKAGE (e.g. ticket) or to allow successive purchases of SALES OFFER PACKAGEs.FARE CONTRACT ENTRYADDITIONALA log entry describing an event referring to the life of a FARE CONTRACT: initial contracting, sales, validation entries, etc. A subset of a FARE CONTRACT ENTRY is often materialised on a TRAVEL DOCUMENT.FARE CONTRACT SECURITY LISTINGADDITIONALThe presence of a FARE CONTRACT on a SECURITY LIST.FARE DAY TYPEBasicA type of day used in the fare collection domain, characterised by one or more properties which affect the definition of access rights and prices in the fare system.FARE DEMAND FACTORBasicA named set of parameters defining a period of travel with a given price, for example off peak, peak, super off peak, etc.FARE ELEMENT INSEQUENCEADDITIONALA FARE ELEMENT as a part of an ELEMENT, including its possible order in the sequence of FARE ELEMENTs.FARE FRAMEBasicThe set of all fare data defined for a specific VEHICLE MODE to which the same VALIDITY CONDITIONs have been assigned.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 366 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version FARE FRAME DEFAULTSBasicSet of pricing parameters and values to apply to an individual element in the frame if no explicit value is specified on the element.FARE INTERVALBasicAn interval based aspect of the fare structure.FARE POINT IN PATTERNBasicA POINT IN PATTERN which represents the start or end of a FARE SECTION, or a point used to define a SERIES CONSTRAINT.FARE PRICEBasicPrice features DEFINED BY DEFAULT characterizing different PRICE GROUPs.FARE PRODUCTBasicAn immaterial marketable element (access rights, discount rights, etc.), specific to a CHARGING MOMENT.FARE PRODUCT PRICEBasicA set of all possible price features of a FARE PRODUCT: default totalprice, discount in value or percentage etc.FARE QUOTA FACTORADDITIONALA named set of parameters defining a number of quota fares available of a given denomination.FARE SCHEDULEDSTOP POINTBasicA specialisation of SCHEDULED STOP POINT describing a stop with fare accounting and routing characteristics.FARE SECTIONBasicA subdivision of a JOURNEY PATTERN consisting of consecutive POINTs IN JOURNEY PATTERN, used to define an element of the fare structure.Fare structureBasicSet of parameters that determine the basic tariffs.FARE STRUCTURE ELEMENTBasicA sequence or set of CONTROLLABLE ELEMENTs to which rules for limitation of access rights and calculation of prices (fare structure) 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 367 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version PARKING CHARGEBANDADDITIONALParking charges that describe the cost of using a PARKING or PARKING AREA for a given period.PARKING PRICEADDITIONALA specialisation of FARE PRICE that defines the price of a PARKING CHARGE BAND.PARKING TARIFFADDITIONALA set of parking CHARGE BANDs that describe the cost of using a PARKING or PARKING AREA.FARE CONTRACTADDITIONALA contract with a particular (but possibly anonymous) customer, ruling the consumption of transport services (and joint services). A FARE CONTRACT may be designed for a fixed SALES OFFER PACKAGE (e.g. ticket) or to allow successive purchases of SALES OFFER PACKAGEs.FARE CONTRACT ENTRYADDITIONALA log entry describing an event referring to the life of a FARE CONTRACT: initial contracting, sales, validation entries, etc. A subset of a FARE CONTRACT ENTRY is often materialised on a TRAVEL DOCUMENT.PENALTY POLICYADDITIONALPolicy regarding different aspects of penalty charges, for example repeated entry at the same station, not having a ticket etc.Post-paid ticketingBasicThe user is charged sometime after using the transport service PRE-ASSIGNED FARE PRODUCTBasicA FARE PRODUCT consisting of one or several VALIDABLE ELEMENTs, specific to a CHARGING MOMENT.Prepaid ticketingBasicThe user is charged for either a fare product (ticket) or a deposit prior to riding (detailed description of process see below).120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 371 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version SALES OFFER ENTITLEMENT GIVENFurtherParameter indicating whether a particular SALES OFFER PACKAGE provides an entitlement to buy or use an access right.SALES OFFER ENTITLEMENT REQUIREDFurtherParameter indicating whether a particular SALES OFFER PACKAGE requires an entitlement to by or use an access right.SALES OFFER PACKAGEBasicA package to be sold as a whole, consisting of one or several FARE PRODUCTs materialised thanks to one or several TRAVEL DOCUMENTs. EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version Annex C(informative)315428Related EU DirectiveITS Directive Delegated Regulation: EU-wide Multimodal Travel Information ServicesThe ITS Directive is a framework for the coordinated and effective deployment and use of intelligent transport systems. Its main objectives are: Establish a framework for coordinated and effective deployment and use of ITS within MS and across bordersDevelop specifications necessary to ensure the compatibility, interoperability and continuity for the deployment and operational use of ITS for priority actionsThe ITS directive defines the following Priority Action.a)the provision of EU-wide multimodal travel information servicesb)the provision of EU-wide real-time traffic information services; c)data and procedures for the provision, where possible, of road safety related minimum universal trafficinformation free of charge to users; d)the harmonised provision for an interoperable EU-wide eCalle)the provision of information services for safe and secure parking places for trucks and commercial vehicles; 120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 382 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu

EUROPEAN UNION AGENCY FOR RAILWAYSTelematics TSI - Technical document – B16Version f)the provision of reservation services for safe and secure parking places for trucks and commercial vehicles.This document is designed to answer to the expectations of Priority Action a) which is expecting the definition of the necessary requirements to make EU-wide multimodal travel information services accurate and available across borders based on one or more of the following types of provisions:functional provisions describing the roles of various stakeholders and the information flow between themtechnical provisions providing for the technical means to fulfil functional provisionsorganisational provisions describing procedural obligations of the various stakeholdersservice provisions describing the levels of services and their contentThe follow are two excerpt article of this commission delegated regulation (eu) of 31.5.2017(17) To ensure the optimal use and full interoperability of the aforementioned standards between Member States, a common minimum profile that identifies the different key elements of the standardshould be established and used within national access points. Member States' national profiles must bebased on a common minimum European profile when it exists.(25) Through the Connecting Europe Facility the Commission will support different technical requirements established within this Regulation through a programme support action19, notably the establishment of the national access point, the conversion to prescribed data exchange standards and the use of common minimum profiles within national access points and the linkage of travel information services where relevant.120 Rue Marc Lefrancq | BP 20392 | FR-59307 Valenciennes CedexPage 383 of 384Tel. +33 (0)327 09 65 00  |  era.europa.eu
